# MEMORY.md

Durable OpenWaggle project memory. Keep this compact and technical. Do not add personal/cross-repo agent preferences here.

## Active Warnings

- The working tree may contain another agent's active refactor. Treat dirty files as intended future shape unless there is clear evidence otherwise.
- Legacy vendor-specific agent configuration has been removed; keep this repository centered on `AGENTS.md` and `.agents/`.
- Legacy agent memory files were removed. Add durable OpenWaggle memory here or to focused skills.
- The Docker token benchmark (`scripts/benchmark-docker/run.sh`) is a supported rerun since v1: the proxy streams chunks once and binds loopback (the Linux bridge gateway on Linux); every local container can reach either, so it takes the real key from its environment (never argv), forwards only requests carrying a per-run token (`BENCH_PROXY_TOKEN`, the agents' API key), and only the benchmark's model endpoints and model, every agent's base URL carries `/agent/<name>` so the proxy attributes each request to its agent (a background title call that finishes after its container exits once landed in the next agent's slice), and each record carries `seq`, its arrival order, which the readers sort by: records are written when a response ends, so a short title call can precede the first model call in the file, and `BENCH_OPENWAGGLE_APP=<OpenWaggle.app>` adds the packaged app itself as the OpenWaggle row through the same proxy. The v1 rerun (2026-10-28, GLM 5.3 flash, first-call prompt tokens): OpenWaggle 4,175 (3,665 o200k prefix, 7 tools), Pi CLI 1.4.0 1,395, opencode 1.19.4 7,035, Codex 0.164.0 8,799, Claude Code 2.1.311 12,766. The website page keeps only these rerun rows (decision 6: as-shipped empty-project prefix <= 4k o200k and below current competitors other than Pi). After a run, `results/` holds the new raw output; strip host-specific warnings from `*.err` before committing.
- `docs/agents/` is reserved for the adapted `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` workflow. Do not manually scaffold it during unrelated work.

## Current Architecture Direction

### CI is tiered behind a merge queue (ADR 0029, September 2026 — superseded by ADR 0033; see "Merge queue removed" below. Retained for history.)

A 2026-09-01 audit of ~300 CI runs (~36h) found 51% green / 21% failed / 27% cancelled, with 85% of failures concentrated on three agent PRs. The enforced merge gate was only three ubuntu checks; the 3-OS E2E matrix and package rehearsals ran informationally per push, so agents burned multi-hour loops on reds that never gated merges. Windows E2E failed 33% of runs — two-thirds of failures were "all 29 tests pass, then `Worker teardown timeout of 90000ms`" (a hanging shutdown, not a test problem), the rest Windows-timing test timeouts and locator misses. `hive-sessions.e2e.test.ts` on PR #181 failed 8× consecutively on a JSON parse race: `applicationCliStdout` returns raw stdout when its extraction grammar does not match, so a leading `{}` empty payload plus trailing content explodes in `JSON.parse`.

Fixes shipped: Fast gate per push (static checks split into Unit / Integration & Component / MCP Conformance jobs + macOS E2E with `retries: 2` and `PLAYWRIGHT_WORKERS: '2'`), Full gate on `merge_group` results (adds Windows/Linux E2E plus path-scoped rehearsals: package consumer smoke when `packages/**`/lockfile/release tooling changed; website/docs rehearsal when website/docs **or package** surfaces changed). `scripts/package-release-gate.ts` encodes tier semantics (`full|fast|fast-no-e2e|visual`); required-but-skipped is an error, skipped-conditional is fine. `e2e/support/electron-process-tree.ts` bounds `app.close()` at 10s, names surviving descendants into `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`, and force-kills the tree (`taskkill /T /F` on Windows) — a safety net whose forensics feed the root-cause hunt for non-clean Windows exits. The settings-side half (enable merge queue + update the required-check list to the new job names) is a maintainer runbook step in `docs/release-and-versioning.md`. It must be applied in the same admin window as the merge: the rename retires the `Unit & Component Tests` context, so between merge and ruleset swap open PRs show a forever-pending required check until an admin applies the swap or merges with the routine bypass.

Adding a reusable CI job requires a deliberate update to the stable job-name list,
caller AST contract, and exact local-workflow reference allowlist. Validate the callee's
AST through the production file validator too, including its immutable SHA preflight,
checkout, permissions, and required commands. Do not exempt reusable jobs generically
or accept their success without the selected gate dependency and result check.
### Merge queue removed (ADR 0033, September 2026, refines ADR 0029)

Releasing a feature felt slow mainly because the merge queue re-ran the Full gate (Windows + Linux Electron E2E + path-scoped rehearsals) on the speculative merge result (~15–25 min), on top of the PR run and the `push: main` run — and Windows E2E is the flakiest gate (~33% teardown-timeout rate). Decision: drop the `merge_queue` rule from the `main` ruleset so PRs merge directly once the required per-PR checks pass; leave all workflow code and release guards unchanged. A full t3code-style rewrite (lean 3-job CI + tag/nightly release) was scoped and rejected as-is: it is incompatible with OpenWaggle's deliberately security-hardened, test-pinned release machinery — `ci.yml`'s fail-closed supply-chain contract (`package-release-validator-*.ts` inside `pnpm check`) requires the classify/prepare/candidate/gate jobs + SLSA provenance attestation that `package-release.yml` consumes, and `release.yml`'s human-approved app-release flow is enforced by `app-release-workflow.unit.test.ts` (never pushes to main, never auto-merges, publishes only a verified human-merged commit). Gutting either to fit a leaner shape removes real security for velocity; right-sizing the package-release apparatus (heavy for a v0.1 alpha with no external consumers) is a legitimate separate change deferred to its own decision. Consequence of removal: main can briefly go red from a semantic conflict between two green PRs. Follow-ups in the same PR: the Release Please version-bump PR (no source changes) now skips the app suite (unit/integration/component/MCP) via an identity-scoped job condition (`!(pull_request && head_ref == 'release-please--branches--main' && head.repo.full_name == github.repository)`, so a fork PR cannot name its way into a skip), accepted by a new fail-closed gate tier `release-pr` that still requires commit policy + `check` + the package candidate (so `prepare-package-release` still attests tarballs). Adding that skip required registering the three test jobs in `QUEUE_ONLY_JOB_CONDITIONS`, decoupling the blocking-job-contract `+if` allowance from the special-runner check in `release-ci-policy.ts`, setting the exact-release-branch `==` guard count to 6, and regenerating `CI_WORKFLOW_AST_CONTRACT` — the normal process for an intentional ci.yml change. Provenance attestation and human-approved releases stay intact. Also in the same PR: the entire Electron E2E suite (`e2e/`, `playwright.config.ts`, the three `electron-e2e-*` jobs) is removed — evidence from `pingdotgg/t3code` is that a shipping multi-platform Electron product runs CI on vitest unit+integration only and has no E2E (its `playwright-core` is an in-app browser-preview feature, not tests). OpenWaggle's 3-OS E2E was the dominant flake (Windows ~33% teardown-timeout). Per-PR gating is now commit-policy + static + unit + integration/component + MCP; the fail-closed gate/policy/AST-hash/tests were updated to drop the E2E jobs (kept the Playwright deps: `playwright-core` powers browser preview, `@playwright/test` powers package browser smoke + website screenshots + QA). A coverage audit (`docs/agents/e2e-removal-coverage-map.md`) found the E2E suite was almost entirely belt-and-suspenders over existing unit/integration/component coverage, so no functional behavior leaves the gate; the nightly cross-OS packaged canary (`.github/workflows/nightly.yml`) covers packaged build + native runtime load + the macOS syntax budget, while renderer boot/CSP/visual/timing have no automated CI control (local/manual only). `e2e/support/session-fixtures.ts` (used by QA seeding + website screenshots) was relocated to `scripts/support/` with an eslint override, since lint never covered `e2e/`.

### The commit policy was invisible to agents

Manual CI has no pull-request base SHA. Resolve its exact candidate's merge base
with the fetched `origin/main` before invoking Commit Policy, and fail on missing
or ambiguous ancestry. Queue runs use their event `merge_group.base_sha`. An empty
`--from` falls back to bootstrap history and loses
the ancestry needed to recognize already-released upstream package syncs.

`scripts/check-conventional-commits.ts` rejected a `mockup:` subject in CI after the agent pushed — the check was deterministic, ~1s, and documented nowhere agents read. Now `pnpm verify` (commit policy vs the `origin/main` merge base + typecheck + lint + unit tests) runs in the husky pre-push hook for feature branches; `prepush:main` still guards pushes to `main`.

- OpenWaggle is an Electron desktop coding-agent UI on top of Pi.
- Main-process architecture is hexagonal: domain, ports, adapters, application services, IPC, stores.
- Pi SDK imports belong in `src/main/adapters/pi/` only.
- Provider/model/auth metadata must mirror Pi through `ModelRuntime`, project-scoped runtime services, and OpenWaggle-owned ports.
- OpenWaggle must not maintain a parallel `src/main/providers/` registry.
- OpenWaggle extension UI direction is ADR-0006: model visual contributions as surface/runtime/execution, default to a framework-neutral federated-module runtime with `mount(context)`, and do not expand placeholder route/content experiments as a parallel legacy runtime.

## Pi Runtime Memory

- Pi 1.0 built-ins (`codemode`, `tool_search`) load through `pi-built-in-extensions.ts` as `builtin:<name>` replaceable inline extensions, like the Pi CLI, so Pi's `-builtin:<name>` still disables one. Automation sets `noExtensions`, which skips built-ins, so automation names them as explicit `builtin:` extension paths. `OPENWAGGLE_AUTOMATION_PI_EXTENSIONS` (path-delimited) loads named QA fixture extensions under automation; jiti caches a fixture module per process, so restart the QA app after editing one.
- OpenWaggle settings with a Pi equivalent (`codemodeEnabled`, `cacheWarming`) are overlaid on Pi's global settings layer in memory (`openwaggle-pi-settings-app-overlay.ts`) and stripped on write when Pi left them as reported. OpenWaggle first, then Pi: `Settings.piRuntimeSettingsChosen` records which keys the user stored; `openWagglePiAppSettingsFrom` reads the global Pi `settings.json` again (`piUserAppSettings`) for the others each time a runtime is built, then OpenWaggle's default (the store also resolves them at load, for display, through `configurePiUserRuntimeSettingsReader`, installed by `runtime-pi-integration.ts`); the overlay appends `+codemode`/`-codemode` last in `defaultTools`, so the effective choice wins over the global list. Cache warming `idle` needs the Run's Pi session to outlive the Run: `pi-idle-cache-warming.ts` keeps it as a warming session (extensions shut down, so no MCP connection; never reused for a Run) while Pi's warmer is active, at most 8 and 31 min. It is ended before anything opens the Session's Pi file again (`createPiRunSessionRuntime`, `withPiSession`, `createPiSessionRuntime`), by the Session removal hook (`runtime.ts`, before archive or deletion touches the file), on Host stop, and when the authoritative `cacheWarming` leaves `idle` (Pi's SettingsManager keeps the value it was built with); context-usage and snapshot reads borrow it instead of opening the file. It also ends as soon as the Pi file's size or mtime differs from what the Run and its own refreshes left (poll, `borrow`, and a guard wrapped around the session's `appendUsage`, whose throw Pi's warmer swallows): a refresh is parented on the warming session's stale leaf, so after `pi --resume` appended, it would become the last entry and resume the old branch. Its `usage` entries alone leave the Pi-file freshness check `unchanged` (`readSnapshot` gets `projectedPiEntryCount`), so warming never re-projects or reorders the sidebar. The same usage-only rule (`onlyUsageAppendedAfter`) keeps the resume position valid after refreshes; `resumeSelectedPosition` then leaves the leaf alone when it follows the selected node through `usage` entries only, so the next message chains onto them as in Pi (branching back made the refresh a sibling and the tree reported a second branch). Branch derivation (`branch-derivation-nodes.ts`) also drops `usage` nodes that end a path and maps an active node on one to the conversation node above, which repairs older files on their next snapshot. Each Run re-applies the warming mode its runtime resolves (`prepareRun`), since a Pi `settings.json` change reaches no settings listener. `installPiRuntimeIntegration` runs from `initializeAppRuntime` and the in-process agents/MCP CLIs, never on import. Faux tests need `promptCache` on the model, costs, a reply queued per refresh, and a `cache_warming_decision` handler returning `warm`.
- Virtual models: assistant messages name the physical model; `session.model` is the virtual one. Look context windows up by the response's provider/model (`modelRuntime.getPhysicalModel`) and tag `context_usage` with `routedFrom` so the meter, which filters by the selected model, accepts it. The renderer provider store must keep `ModelDisplayInfo.virtual`; it rebuilds model records field by field. Pi's `session.routedModel` names the latest response even if it came from before a switch, so projection tags each assistant node's metadata with `selectedModel` (nearest `model_change` on its path, `entry-model-selection.ts`) and the routed label only counts responses made under the current selection.
- pi-tui `Input.setValue` keeps the cursor where it was (start for a new Input); prefilled inputs enter the value as a bracketed paste so typing appends. `parseKey(' ')` is `"space"`, so a plain character must be taken as itself before `parseKey`.

- Pi 0.84.4 emits `agent_end` with `willRetry: true` for a transient assistant error, then `auto_retry_start`. If the retry delay is stopped, Pi emits `auto_retry_end` with `finalError: "Retry cancelled"`; this is cancellation, not a provider failure. Preserve `willRetry` and cancellation separately in transport events, and give the run's aborted state priority over any error retained in Pi history. Pi's default HTTP idle timeout is five minutes; Undici may report a stalled or closed streaming connection as `terminated`, which Pi considers retryable. Classify that as a provider connection failure while retaining the raw detail.

Load `.agents/skills/pi-integration/SKILL.md` for details.

- Prompt cache and Bedrock patches (v1 performance, October 2026; each has a test in `adapters/pi/__tests__/`). The pi-ai Bedrock transport keeps one `BedrockRuntimeClient` per configuration (region, endpoint, profile, user agent, hashed static credentials or bearer token, request handler; LRU 8, closed after 10 min idle), so the SSO credential fetch and the HTTP/2 session happen once: time to first byte went from ~1.25 s to ~0.69 s per call on EU Haiku. A client unused for 30 s closes its handler's connections before its next request (a silently dead pooled connection would hang it until TCP gives up), pooled HTTP/2 sessions keep the SDK's 300 s idle bound, and a request AWS refuses outright (`UnrecognizedClientException`, bad signature, access denied) drops the cached clients without a retry, since the SDK memoizes static credentials per client. The tools cache point is written for Claude model references only, even under `AWS_BEDROCK_FORCE_CACHE`. Per-request middleware goes on the command, never the shared client. Pi's cache warmer reads `model.promptCache`, and the generated Bedrock catalog had none, so `cacheWarming: streaming` never kept a Bedrock cache alive during long tools or `hive wait` (most of the 5-minute TTL misses); `amazon-bedrock.models.js` now declares them for the Claude models Bedrock caches, the warmer derives them for Bedrock models from other catalogs, and budget-thinking Claude on Bedrock is not replayed (its budget follows `maxTokens`). Bedrock gets four cache points: tools, the system prompt before section `cwd` (`systemCacheBoundary`, set by Pi's SDK), the rest of the system prompt, and the last user message; a second Session with another tail read 17.7k of 18k prefix tokens from cache. Per-Session text therefore belongs after `cwd` ("The first-turn prefix is a budget" below). Collapsed tools keep the leading system message's order and sort later additions by name, so a reconnecting MCP server no longer reorders them. Bash shows the model at most 16 KB (first and last 8 KB); codemode no longer appends "resolves to a string" to every declared tool.

- Agents learn about the installed OpenWaggle docs bundle (`Resources/openwaggle-docs`, dev `build/openwaggle-docs` from `pnpm docs:generate`) only through the Pi-native `openwaggle_docs` tool (`openwaggle-docs-tool-extension.ts`, list/search/read over `listDocsDiscoveryView`, global and Session-project extension docs included). Its `promptGuidelines` carry the pointer, and name the bundle root only when `index.json` loaded for the Run (`availableBundlePath`); a missing bundle never fails a Run, the tool returns an error naming the expected path. Discovery lists every extension's docs whatever its trust, and only checks containment when it hashes the file (a symlink out of the package keeps its path with `contentHash: null`). The tool therefore offers only extensions the Run loads (`listRuntimeEnabledPackagePaths`, which also honors the project override) whose docs have a non-null hash, and `read` re-checks the realpath against the package and the hash before returning content; any other consumer that reads extension doc content needs the same checks. Pi's own `<docs>` block points into `app.asar` in packaged builds: Electron's asar fs patch lets Pi's `read` and `ls` tools open those files, but shell commands and the spawned `find`/`grep` tools (fd, rg) get "Not a directory", so the guideline points at the bundle's plain-file `topics/pi/` copies.

- Since Pi 1.2.0, `sendCustomMessage({ triggerTurn: true })`, which starts every Waggle turn, prepares the run as `prompt()` does (`_prepareRun`: `before_agent_start`, prompt sections, tool loadout, kept on the turn's later requests; `BeforeAgentStartEvent` is then `messageType: "custom"` with `message`, not `prompt`). Pi 1.0.4 did not, so OpenWaggle's patch had `_runCustomMessageTurn`; on 1.2.0 the patch keeps only `_prepareNewTurn` (auth refresh and the pre-prompt compaction check, after `before_agent_start`) and the failed-preparation run: a custom-message preparation that throws still ends as an error turn and `agent_end` (`PreparedRunError` in `_startRun`), since `pi.sendMessage` only reports its rejection to the extension error listener, which `bindSessionExtensions` logs; a Waggle Run fails on it (`onExtensionTurnStartFailure` ends the loop through its `fail`), a classic Run does not, since Pi finishes its turn itself. Once `before_agent_start` has run, the failed turn keeps the turn's prompt and loadout, so Pi's automatic retry sends them. pi-waggle advances a turn on `agent_settled`, not `agent_end`, which Pi also emits for a failed request it is about to retry, after overflow compaction, and before a queued continuation, each with only that attempt's messages: `onPiWaggleSettledAgentEnd` (`settled-run.ts`) gathers every `agent_end` of the run and drops failed attempts a later response replaced; the default extension a Pi TUI user loads uses it too, with a pi-ai classifier (`default-turn-error.ts`), which imports pi-ai lazily: pi-ai is ESM-only and the package's CommonJS build (checked by the package consumer smoke in `pnpm check`) must load without it. OpenWaggle runtimes never load an installed `@openwaggle/pi-waggle` package (`pi-waggle-package-dedupe.ts`, `extensionsOverride`): its default extension filters every Waggle request and turn message out of `context` and would run a second loop. pi-waggle's stop policy takes a host error classifier (`summarizePiWaggleTurnMessages(messages, { isRecoverableError })`); OpenWaggle's (`waggle-turn-error.ts`) uses Pi's `isRetryableAssistantError` and `isContextOverflow`, so an error no later turn recovers from (rejected sign-in, missing model, quota or billing, uncompactable overflow) stops the collaboration and the Run ends with it, while transient errors stay recoverable. A failed response's `errorMessage` is hydrated as `Message.metadata.error` (not for an attempt a `context_edit(…, null)` drops: `omittedFromContext`), live too through `message_end.errorMessage`; a Waggle turn row shows the error of its last response only (`withWaggleTurnFailures`), and the Run's last failed turn leaves it to the Run error row while that shows. A response takes its Waggle turn from the nearest `pi-waggle.turn` past earlier responses, tool results and context edits (`waggle-turn-metadata.ts`) (`docs/pi-runtime-upgrades.md`; upstream bug to report). `before_agent_start` therefore fires on every Waggle turn: OpenWaggle's handler only sets sections and the tool filter, and Pi's MCP handler waits once (bounded) for direct servers to connect and sets `mcp_servers`, so both are idempotent; the Run identity is decided once per Run in `context`. `waggle-turn-system-prompt.integration.test.ts` checks it with real Pi, a file-backed session, and a faux tool call.
- Pi 1.2.0 run contract (checked in the 1.2.0 upgrade): the session counts as busy from the moment a run starts preparing, `before_agent_start` included, so `ctx.isIdle()` is false there, `pi.sendMessage()` queues into the starting run, `pi.sendUserMessage()` then needs `deliverAs`, and an abort requested meanwhile aborts the run once it exists. `agent_settled` carries `aborted` (1.1.0) and `tool_execution_end` `durationMs`. OpenWaggle's `before_agent_start` handler only writes sections and narrows tools, so running it for custom-message runs too adds nothing twice; pi-waggle's `input` handler checks `isIdle()` before any run starts, and its default extension sends its continuation prompt a tick after the run settled. Pi no longer lists MCP resources on connect (`pi mcp list --json` says `resources: true`); OpenWaggle's inspection lists tools only and MCP Apps read `ui://` resources directly, so neither depended on it.
- Pi extension commands (`pi.registerCommand`) run when a message starts with `/name`: Pi's `prompt()` runs them instead of prompting the model. The slash menu lists a Session's commands through `agent:list-extension-commands` (Host UI revision 24): the warm session's or a read-only Pi session's, plus those its latest Run had (`run-extension-commands.ts`, recorded in `createPiSessionForRun`), since some extensions load only in a Run. Pi's MCP `/mcp` is deliberately not registered: it is a terminal menu, and OpenWaggle manages MCP in Settings > MCP (`forOpenWaggleMcpSurface`, `mcp-openwaggle-surface.ts`, which also rewrites Pi's "Run /mcp to fix/sign in" and "Run /login <provider>" wording in notices and tool results to name Settings > MCP / Settings > Connections). A first message that invokes one is not used for the title (`invokesExtensionCommand` in the Run preflight).
- Pi 1.0 prompt caching: never return `systemPrompt` from `before_agent_start` for per-Session context. Mutate `event.systemPromptOptions.sections` instead (`agent-run-context-extension.ts` writes `openwaggle_agent_definition` and `openwaggle_session_identity`), and keep those sections byte-stable across Runs. Changing a section mid-Session does not protect the cache on most providers: `supportsMidConvoSystemMessages` defaults to false, so pi-ai collapses every later system-message patch into the leading prompt (`collapseSystemMessages`). Values that change per Run (runId, Session title, Hive role, Worker count, Delegation state) therefore go in the `openwaggle-run-identity` Run identity. It is stored once per Run, only when it differs from the latest one, as a plain `custom` entry (`pi.appendEntry`), never a `custom_message`, so `pi --resume` never sends OpenWaggle's Run metadata; it is written once per Run on the Run's first `context` call, whose messages hold the message that started the Run, and names it: the latest user message or `pi-waggle.user-request` after the last answer (never a Waggle turn message, since every turn adds one), else the first message after the last answer, such as the custom message of an extension command's `pi.sendMessage(..., { triggerTurn: true })`. A user message is matched by timestamp and content digest; a custom message by custom type and digest, since reloaded from the file it carries its entry's time; each identity is placed by branch order: it counts the matching prompt entries the branch sent after it (k) and takes the k-th-from-last matching message, so an older identity never moves onto a later identical message without one (a pi CLI turn) and an identity whose message was compacted away is dropped (`agent-run-identity.ts`); the `context` handler sends each stored identity as a hidden custom message right after that prompt, where Pi used to put the message, so earlier Runs' request prefixes and their prompt cache stay the same. Never place it by time: Pi's entries and messages are stamped in milliseconds, and a fast Run puts the identity, prompt, answer, and next Run's identity in one millisecond (the first time-based version misplaced it about one run in four). Older files keep their `custom_message` identities. The Session tree hides the entry (`node-hydration.ts`). `session-run-execution-profile.ts` splits `identityContext` (Session-level) from `runIdentityContext` (Run-scoped). `agent-run-context-prompt-cache.integration.test.ts` uses a real Pi session to check that the leading prompt, raw and collapsed, stays identical across Runs.

- Pi JSONL sessions are runtime state; SQLite session projection is the product read model for renderer navigation, branching, persistence, active runs, and UI state.
- Pi 1.0 nested tool calls (`ctx.executeTool()`, e.g. codemode) emit `tool_execution_*` with `parentToolCallId` and ids `<parent>/<n>`, but get no transcript entry; Pi keeps only a bounded `nestedCalls` record (no results) on the calling tool's `toolResult` message. OpenWaggle forwards `parentToolCallId` on transport events, never turns a nested call into a part (both drop ones with no known owner), and persists Pi's record as `ToolCallResult.nestedCalls` so the card's nested list survives reload. Live lists (renderer `ChatToolCallPart.nestedCalls` and the active-run buffer's `ToolCallRequest.nestedCalls`, for reconnects) are built by `shared/utils/nested-tool-calls.ts`, which mirrors Pi's `NESTED_CALL_LIMITS` (256 calls, 8/32 KiB args, 500 error chars, no results) and counts drops in `droppedCalls`; Pi's record only says `complete: false`, so a full incomplete record means dropped calls. Owners match by `<ownerId>/` prefix, so calls made by dropped calls still find their owner. A ts-match pattern over two `UIMessagePart`s exceeds TypeScript's instantiation depth once parts carry recursive JSON args; narrow by hand there. Pi writes codemode images and truncated output to `os.tmpdir()` (`utils/output-files.js`).
- Pi-native tool events, thinking levels, compaction behavior, session ids, provider/model ids, and auth methods should stay Pi-native through the adapter boundary.
- Composer thinking choices must come from Pi's `getSupportedThinkingLevels(model)` result. Render that array exactly: `Off`, `Extra High`, and `Max` appear only when Pi declares them for the selected model; never infer levels from model ids.
- Missing projected Pi entries during clean-cut projection rebuilds should be treated as stale/cancelled navigation, not thrown through IPC.
- Preserve Pi-created session ids before first prompt by opening the pre-created id correctly instead of allowing a missing JSONL path to create a different id.
- Build runtime services through Pi's project-scoped service path so extensions/providers are registered before model resolution.
- Pi package extension loading must be scoped to the active project and adapter cwd so package extensions do not read Electron's process cwd or leak server processes.
- OpenWaggle masks user-managed `pi-mcp-adapter` npm entries from its embedded Pi SettingsManager at read time and restores them on writes; never uninstall or remove those shared entries because standalone Pi and other projects may still use them.
- OpenWaggle-owned Pi extension packages must be bundled/copied locally and `asarUnpack`ed for packaged apps.
- Pi lazy-loads some Node-only modules through a bundler-opaque variable-specifier dynamic import (e.g. the Amazon Bedrock Converse impl and the OAuth flow loaders) so browser/Bun bundlers cannot pull in the Node-only deps. OpenWaggle bundles pi-ai into the Electron main chunk with `codeSplitting: false`, so those specifiers resolve next to `out/main/index.js` where no chunk was emitted, failing at runtime with `Cannot find module .../out/main/<name>.js` — packaged-only, and only when that provider/flow is actually exercised. The fix is Pi's static-override escape hatch, registered before first use: `registerPiBundledOAuthFlows()` (`pi-bundled-oauth.ts`, `setBedrockProviderModule`→`pi-bundled-bedrock.ts`), called at `runtime.ts` module load AND at the ModelRuntime construction chokepoint in `pi-provider-catalog.ts` (idempotent). Every other provider uses a literal-string dynamic import that rolldown inlines fine — Bedrock was the last opaque one. Any new Pi lazy loader needs the same registration or it regresses the same way.
- Pi 1.0 adds more bundle-sensitive paths, all behind OpenWaggle's `PI_BUNDLED_NODE` define. Codemode starts its QuickJS sandbox from `./codemode-worker.js` next to the main bundle (read into a `data:` URL, so it may import only `node:` builtins), and image resizing starts `./image-resize-worker.js` beside the resizing module (falling back to the main thread when it cannot): `scripts/vite/pi-worker-bundles.ts` emits both after every main build (codemode as one ES module, the image worker as CommonJS). WASM is read from installed packages, so both are root dependencies pinned to Pi's exact versions: `quickjs-wasi` (resolved with `createRequire(<bundle>)`; `check:pi-runtime` enforces one lockfile version) and `@silvia-odwyer/photon-node`, which reads `photon_rs_bg.wasm` next to its own CJS entry. Bundled, Photon found no wasm, so `read` returned "Read image file [image/png]" text instead of an image block and codemode `image(await tools.read(...))` failed; keeping Photon external fixed it. Pi also finds its README, docs (codemode points the model at `docs/codemode.md`), examples, themes, export templates, and `VERSION` by walking up from `import.meta.url` to the nearest package.json, which in the bundle is OpenWaggle's (Pi reported OpenWaggle's version and pointed at `app.asar/README.md`). `src/main/apply-pi-package-directory.ts` (imported near the top of index.ts, before any Pi module evaluates) always sets Pi's own `PI_PACKAGE_DIR` to the installed package, even over a user's value for another Pi install, and `withShellPiPackageDirectory` gives agent bash/PowerShell, terminal shells, `getEnvWithOverrides` children (stdio MCP servers, git with overrides, the updater), and the detached Session Host's launch environment the user's own value back (or none); a spawn that inherits `process.env` implicitly still sees OpenWaggle's value, so new child-process code should build its environment through the env helpers, so a standalone `pi` still finds its own package and the Host sets its value again. Packaging Pi's README, docs, and examples needs two things: file patterns re-including them after the generic `node_modules/**/docs` exclusion, and `onNodeModuleFile` (`scripts/electron-builder-pi-assets.ts`), because electron-builder's `NodeModuleCopyHelper` drops every module's top-level README and `examples` before file patterns apply. `packaged-app:smoke` asserts them against the real asar; without the hook it fails on README.md and examples while the source-tree bundle smoke still passes. `pi-mcp` is bundled like the other Pi packages; the MCP runtime and CLI and the codemode executor use literal lazy imports that Rolldown inlines. `pnpm pi-main-bundle:smoke` (part of `pnpm check`; about 7 s locally, where the whole `pnpm check` took 262 s in October 2026, against CI Typecheck & Lint runs of 231–360 s on main and a 20-minute job timeout) builds an entry with the real main config and, under Node and Electron-as-Node, checks Pi's package assets and `VERSION`, reads a PNG as an image block, resizes through the bundled image worker, and runs a codemode script that calls an MCP tool from a stdio server and shows the read image. OAuth loaders, including Pi 0.99's `openai-chatgpt` and `radius`, stay covered by `registerBunOAuthFlows`.
- Pi supplies `typebox` to extensions along with its SDKs (Pi `docs/packages.md`, "Declare dependencies"), and since 0.99.0 it warns on every start when a Pi package lists one of them under `dependencies`. The unreleased pi-waggle manifest listed `typebox` there from #153, after 0.1.1 was published, until #339; only loading the packed package in the real Pi TUI showed it, because unit tests, the package consumer smoke and the desktop bundle never ran Pi's resource-loader manifest check. The consumer smoke now loads the tarball through `DefaultResourceLoader` and fails on its warnings. `assertPiHostPackagesArePeers` in the package consumer smoke now enforces `"*"` peers, and `docs/pi-waggle-release-checklist.md` keeps the manual TUI pass in the release routine. Driving the TUI non-interactively works with a pty and a terminal emulator (Python `pyte`), `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` pointed at a throwaway directory, and a local OpenAI-compatible stub in its `models.json`, which runs a full two-turn Waggle with no credentials.
- Pi-native Waggle state belongs to `@openwaggle/pi-waggle`: runtime custom message/state types use the `pi-waggle.*` namespace, branch mode/config is stored as `pi-waggle.mode-state` custom entries, and OpenWaggle should project metadata from those entries instead of seeding a parallel metadata tree.
- Pi TUI Waggle continuation turns should be scheduled after the current Pi run settles, then append the visible `pi-waggle.turn` custom message and call `sendUserMessage(..., { deliverAs: 'followUp' })`. Pi ignores `deliverAs` while idle, which it is after settling, and Pi 1.2 refuses `sendUserMessage` without it while a run is starting (busy-while-starting); never queue continuation prompts during `turn_end`, which can leave them waiting for user confirmation. Pi 1.1+ settles a run the user aborted (Escape) with `agent_settled.aborted`: `onPiWaggleSettledAgentEnd` then drops the run's messages and calls `onAborted` instead of judging the turn; `createPiWaggleExtension` resolves `done` there, and the default extension restores the inherited model and says the Waggle stopped. Stop pressed in the `setTimeout(0)` gap between turns aborts no Pi run, so nothing settles as aborted: the default scheduler's `canStartNextTurn()` check then calls `onComplete`, or `done` (which OpenWaggle's Waggle Run awaits before its `finally` and durable active-run cleanup) would never settle. The default extension advances on `agent_settled` (`onPiWaggleSettledAgentEnd`), with every `agent_end` of the settled run gathered and attempts a later response replaced dropped; never on `turn_end`, which also ends a failed attempt Pi is about to retry. Its pi-ai turn-error classifier loads lazily: a literal `import()` in the ES module build (which Pi's extension module mapping serves), and in the CommonJS build, where TypeScript compiles `import()` to `require()` and pi-ai's `import`-only exports refuse it, a real `import()` built with `Function` (`esm-import.ts`, created on use). It is absent only where pi-ai cannot load at all; a failed load is not cached. The package consumer smoke runs a settled turn from the CommonJS build (`require-waggle-turn-smoke.cjs`).
- User-authored input during an active Pi TUI Waggle run must stop automatic Waggle continuation and be resent with `sendUserMessage(..., { deliverAs: 'steer' })`; otherwise Pi rejects it with “Agent is already processing” because normal prompts during streaming need an explicit streaming behavior.
- Pi custom TUI components must use `@earendil-works/pi-tui` keyboard helpers such as `matchesKey`/`parseKey` instead of raw escape-sequence comparisons; Kitty keyboard protocol encodes Enter, Esc, Space, and Ctrl+C as CSI-u sequences, so raw checks can trap users inside custom menus.
- Pi custom TUI components must never return strings containing embedded `\n`/`\r`; normalize dynamic labels/details to single terminal lines before rendering. They must also truncate rendered lines with `truncateToWidth`/`visibleWidth`, not string length, clamp scroll windows to `items.length - visibleRows`, and reserve fixed blank slots for scrollable lists/details. Embedded newlines, overflowing lines, or shrinking terminal output can corrupt Pi TUI's differential cursor math and leave duplicated-looking rows. Prefer Pi's built-in `ctx.ui.select` for modal menus unless custom rendering is clearly needed, so the Pi footer/status remains visible and interaction steps do not visually jump.

- First-turn injection is measured through OpenWaggle's own Run composition, not bare Pi: `pnpm perf:first-turn-prefix` (in-process, every first-party extension, faux provider, o200k per section and per tool; also the CI ratchet budget in `scripts/perf/first-turn-prefix/first-turn-prefix-budget.ts`; `run-composition.ts` builds the Run with the Host's own builders, `classicRunInlineExtensions`, `createRunSessionsExtension`, `createRunTrustedExtensionFactories` and `runSystemPromptAppendices`, so add Run extensions there, not beside them; the prefix varies by about ±10 between runs because the temporary project path is random) and `pnpm perf:first-turn-prefix:packaged` (a packaged app's real `openwaggle run` request to a local recording provider; `--setup maintainer` adds heavy MCP, skills and AGENTS.md). On beta.27 an empty project's first request was ~13.3k o200k (~22k Opus; about 3.8k after v1, see "The first-turn prefix is a budget" below) and a maintainer-like setup ~32.7k o200k (~54k Opus); the old `benchmark-first-turn-tokens.ts` (1,609 tokens) measured the bare Pi SDK and was removed. Pi still discovers user skills from `~/.agents/skills` (agentskills.io convention) even with a fresh agent dir. Historical (pre-v1) container results, superseded by the v1 rerun in `scripts/benchmark-docker/results/`: Equal-footing OpenRouter/z-ai-glm-5.3-flash comparisons (2026-09-02, pristine pinned Docker containers via `scripts/benchmark-docker/run.sh`, wire-truth from the logging proxy): Pi CLI 0.84.4 1356, OpenWaggle bundled 0.81.1 1576 via its native provider (1319 when routed as a raw openai-completions endpoint), Reasonix 1.35.0 5279 on the wire (its CLI reports 10558 because it re-sends the conversation to a completion validator - report the wire), opencode 1.18.26 7054, Codex CLI 0.150.1 9957 (Responses endpoint needs `model_reasoning_effort` set or OpenRouter rejects the bare `reasoning.summary`), Claude Code 2.1.247 17608 (a run against the real user home measured 22260 - installed plugins alone added ~4650 tokens, which is why the container harness matters; native Bedrock opus via `cc-w` measured 16911). Cross-harness turn-1 injection varies ~13x with the same model. Routing notes: Codex needs a custom `[model_providers.*]` with `wire_api = "responses"` AND `model_reasoning_effort` set, because OpenRouter's Responses endpoint rejects a bare `reasoning.summary`; opencode takes an `@ai-sdk/openai-compatible` provider in an isolated `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`; Gemini CLI 0.31 cannot be routed (auth types are Google-only and the user's Code-Assist-for-individuals tier is deprecated); Cursor CLI is login-walled and speaks Cursor's proprietary protocol, so it cannot be pointed at OpenRouter. A local forwarding proxy in front of OpenRouter is the reliable way to observe per-call prompt_tokens when a harness aggregates or rewrites usage; it must stream chunks through incrementally (buffering whole SSE responses makes agents time out and retry ~13x, which poisons CLI-reported sums). The full reproducible harness (pinned-version Dockerfile, per-agent configs, streaming proxy, results parser) ships in `scripts/benchmark-docker/` and feeds `website/src/data/benchmark-results.json` -> `benchmarks.ts` for the docs page at `/docs/using-openwaggle/token-benchmarks` (a decision demoted the section off the landing page: aider at 587 and DeepSeek Harness sdk-minimal at 1115 beat OpenWaggle's 1609 on turn-1 injection, so no superlative claim is defensible; OpenWaggle stays in the lean tier, 6-11x lighter than Codex and Claude Code). A task-efficiency benchmark (same model, same small self-judging tasks, tokens-per-solved-task, e.g. on aider's exercise set) was scoped and parked as a possible v2. The repository-standards guard forbids the legacy vendor runtime name; benchmark files are recorded as exempt in `scripts/standards/forbidden-references.ts` because the benchmark must name its subjects. Measuring headless agents requires isolated homes: `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR`, temp `CODEX_HOME` with a symlinked `auth.json`, and `--setting-sources`/`--bare` break Claude's OAuth refresh, so only `--disable-slash-commands` plus `--strict-mcp-config` is safe there.
- Pi 1.2.0's Sign in with ChatGPT (`openai-chatgpt`) sends OpenAI a stable installation ID and reads it from `ModelRuntime.login`'s fourth argument, `LoginOptions.getDeviceId`. Without it the flow throws "requires a device ID (UUID) for this installation" before opening the browser. `pi-provider-oauth-service.ts` passes `getDeviceId: () => settingsManager.getOrCreateDeviceId()` from `createOpenWaggleGlobalPiSettingsManager()` and flushes it, so the ID lives in `~/.pi/agent/settings.json` as Pi's own login stores it. `SettingsManager.flush()` never rejects: a failed write is only reported by `drainErrors()`, which the sign-in logs. `LoginOptions.agentName` (OpenAI's agent name hint) is not passed, so OpenAI's consent screen still says "Pi". Check new `LoginOptions` fields on every Pi upgrade.

### The first-turn prefix is a budget (v1 performance, October 2026)

An empty project's first request was 13.3k o200k (22k Opus) before any user text; it is now about 3.8k. What keeps it there: rarely used OpenWaggle tools (`preview_*`, `project_actions`, `source_control`, `openwaggle_docs`) register through `onDemandToolRegistration` as Pi `deferred` tools in the `openwaggle` namespace, announced by one rule each, with no prompt snippets or guidelines (they would change the system prompt when `tool_search` loads them). `hive` declares only its hot actions and keeps the rest behind `help` (`sessions-tool-hot-schema.ts`); Pi coerces only declared properties, so `parseSessionsToolActionArguments` drops optional nulls and runs `Value.Convert` against the action's own variant before checking it (a spread variant loses TypeBox's non-enumerable `~kind` and `Value.Convert` stops descending: copy variants with property descriptors). An Agent definition allowlist that names a deferred OpenWaggle tool gets it declared directly (`allowedToolLoadout`), since the allowlist filters out `tool_search` and codemode; its `<rules>` are `sessionsToolEssentialGuidelines`, the full set is help output. PowerShell is declared only on Windows: Pi's `getPowerShellConfig` throws "only available on Windows" elsewhere, so offering it where `pwsh` was on the PATH (as the first v1 cut did) handed macOS and Linux models a tool that failed on every call. Per-Session text (scratch, evidence and visualization directories, the Session identity) lives in custom sections Pi renders after `cwd`, never in Pi's `addendum` or in `<rules>`, so everything before `cwd` is shared by a project's Sessions (the Bedrock `systemCacheBoundary` cache point in "Prompt cache and Bedrock patches" above). The skills listing is rewritten in `before_agent_start` only (relative/`~` paths, 160-character descriptions that keep the "Use when…" trigger sentence and shorten the opening before it); never change `skill.filePath` in `skillsOverride`, since Pi reads and expands skills from it. Hive results are compacted and capped at 32 KB with the whole result in `<scratch>/hive-results/<toolCallId>-<suffix>.json` (providers reuse ids such as `call_0`); a page always keeps at least one item, cut to a preview when it alone is over the cap, and verbose results page under `response.outcome`.

### Pi entry ids are only unique within one file (ADR 0049)

`session_nodes.id` is a global key and node ids are Pi entry ids. Pi's stock eight-character ids collided across Sessions once the database held ~80k nodes: one reused id made every later snapshot of that Session fail with `UNIQUE constraint failed: session_nodes.id`, shown as "This response couldn't be saved", and the projection stopped at the collision while the Pi file kept everything. The Pi patch now mints full UUIDs. `persistSessionSnapshotWithSql` checks for ids held by other Sessions first and fails with `SessionNodeIdConflictError` (names the id and its owner); the Session repository then renames those entries in the Pi file through the `SessionTranscriptRepair` port (`repairForeignSnapshotEntryIds`) and saves again. The rewrite refuses unparseable files and files that changed meanwhile, and only re-projects changed entries. Anything that reads a node id from the snapshot after saving (turn-checkpoint anchors) must resolve it against the saved tree. Never key anything new by a Pi entry id alone, and scope every `session_nodes` write by `session_id`.

### A Session's recorded Pi file is only a hint until its first run settles

Preparation records the file of a manager it creates in the opened checkout, but Pi writes a file only on the first assistant message, and the first run opens its own manager in the run directory (the worktree, for a worktree Session), so Pi writes a differently named file elsewhere. That file is recorded only when the run's snapshot is saved. A Host that stopped during a Session's first run left it pointing at a file that never existed, and the next run started an empty transcript under the same Pi session id, silently dropping the conversation. `createSessionManagerForSession` now looks for `*_<piSessionId>.jsonl` in the Pi session directories of the run directory, the recorded path, the Session's checkout and its worktree before starting fresh, and logs a warning, once per Session, when a Session that already has an assistant reply still has no file. It does not merge Sessions that already started over on a second file before this fix; the newest file wins. Deleting a Session removes every `*_<piSessionId>.jsonl` in those same directories (`findPiSessionFiles`, through the `SessionTranscriptFiles` port), not only the recorded file; the deletion journal records the newest copy for a Session with no recorded file, because after the Session row is gone that file name is the only record of its Pi session id. A discovered Session's files are never removed this way: a cleanup that journals no staged path keeps the file and every copy (ADR 0051). Discovered nodes carry a `<sessionId>/` prefix, so the ADR 0049 rename never applies to them. The directories come from `piSessionDirectoryFor`, which honours a configured Pi session directory.

- Since Pi 1.0.4 (unchanged in 1.2.0), main's #282 streaming-argument test for OpenAI Responses ends the normal case with `output_item.done` (empty arguments), then `completed`: Pi refuses a toolUse message whose call never got `output_item.done`, so the old ending is now tested as refused, and `finishStreamingToolCalls` runs after that refusal.

### Runs project only what Pi appended (v1 performance D6, October 2026)

`session_nodes.content_hash` (migration 80) is sha256 of `content_json` + `metadata_json`; snapshot persistence selects light columns + hash and reads content only where the hash differs or is null (old rows, filled lazily on the Session's next persist). The reconcile writers keep it; trigger `session_nodes_content_hash_guard` clears a hash another writer left stale. `pi_session_file_projections.tail_*` (migration 81) records where the projected entries end (offset, last line start, digest of the first 64 KiB + that line, entry id, count, leaf), only when the file's last line is the snapshot's last Pi entry. While the file only grew (`piSessionFileStillHasTail`), a Run's snapshot keeps the earlier entries as lazy nodes (`retainedPiEntryNode`, kind/role from the stored rows) marked in a WeakMap with the projection revision (`retained-snapshot-nodes.ts`); `persistPiSessionSnapshotWithSql` trusts them only if that revision is still current and a tail is recorded, and then updates only their tree columns. Entries whose context-edit omission changed are projected again. The tail also records the entry-projection version (`tail_projection_version`, migration 84); `projectPiSessionSnapshot` ignores a base from another version, so bump `PI_ENTRY_PROJECTION_VERSION` whenever `projectPiEntry` output changes, or stored rows never get the fix. Copying a node (`{...node}`) drops its mark (correct, just slower) and forces its lazy content. A refresh reads only the bytes after the tail: usage-only appends are `unchanged` without parsing the file. Pi's own `SessionManager.open` still parses the whole file per Run: its constructor is private and preloading would keep every parsed entry in Host memory between Runs. Measured on a synthetic 95 MB / 16k-entry Session: refresh after a warming append 130 → 0.9 ms, Run projection 145 → 48 ms, existing-node select 117 → 34 ms (0 content bytes read for an unchanged Session).

## MCP Runtime Memory

- Never put keychain access (`safeStorage`, the MCP vault, the encrypted OAuth store) on the Host start path: with a scratch HOME, `safeStorage` stalled the main loop for 90-185 s, and Host start awaited the ADR 0051 migration's sign-in step. File migration (`migrateOpenWaggleMcpFilesOnce`) is keychain-free and awaited at start; the sign-in step (`migrateOpenWaggleMcpSignInsOnce`) starts after the Host listens (`startServicesAfterListening`), and only MCP connect and sign-in paths wait for it. The MCP vault uses `safeStorage`'s async API (`decryptStringAsync` opens `encryptString` output, same `v10` format, verified with `--use-mock-keychain`), and the sign-in step reads the old sign-ins it needs between two queued passes, outside the migration queue and lock (none once recorded), so a stall there leaves the Host responsive. The encrypted OAuth store is still synchronous (Pi's backend contract): moving an old sign-in into it and every OAuth connection block the main loop while the keychain stalls. An overridden profile (`OPENWAGGLE_USER_DATA_DIR`) sets `app.setAppLogsPath(<userData>/logs)` in `configureAppStoragePaths`, so QA logs, the Host's included, stay out of `~/Library/Logs`.
- MCP runs on Pi 1.0's built-in MCP extension (ADR 0051); `docs/mcp-runtime.md` is the map. SDK sessions do not load Pi's built-ins, so `pi-agent-kernel-run.ts` adds `createOpenWaggleMcpExtensions()` (`src/main/adapters/pi/mcp/`) to every Run's `extensionFactories` as the `builtin:mcp` inline extension (replaceable, like `builtin:codemode` and `builtin:tool-search` from `pi-built-in-extensions.ts`), so Pi's `-builtin:mcp` in the `extensions` setting turns MCP off as in the Pi CLI. Like other first-party factories, it is dropped in hidden automation unless `OPENWAGGLE_AUTOMATION_FIRST_PARTY_EXTENSIONS=1`; then `pi-provider-resources.ts` also names `builtin:mcp` explicitly, because `noExtensions` skips built-ins that are not named.
- Pi's MCP extension notifies "MCP servers are still connecting; their tools become available once connected." (`info`) from its `before_agent_start` wait whenever a direct-tool server misses `startupWaitMs`, which in OpenWaggle meant a toast on nearly every new Session. `forOpenWaggleMcpSurface` (`mcp-openwaggle-surface.ts`) wraps the factory and drops only `info` notices from `before_agent_start` handlers; the launch step already shows the server connecting. Failures still notify: Pi's `session_start` reports "MCP servers need attention" (`warning`) once every server's first connect settles, which the wrapper keeps (with its "Run /mcp to fix" line pointed at Settings > MCP); it is lost only when a server fails after the Run already ended (session_shutdown aborts the report). `mcp-openwaggle-surface.integration.test.ts` checks Pi's unwrapped extension still sends the connecting notice (so a Pi upgrade that moves it fails the test) and that a server that fails to connect still warns.
- Pi's plug-in points need symbols Pi 1.2.0 still does not export from its root: `McpOAuthCredentialStore` (the `credentials` option is that class, nominal through its private members), `signInMcpServer`, `createMcpAuthProvider`, `validateMcpServerConfig`, `mcpNamespace`, `getMcpToolExposure`, `resolveConfigValueOrThrow`, `resolveHeadersOrThrow`. The pi-coding-agent patch adds exports only (own commit); re-add them on every Pi upgrade. Without a `credentials` store Pi reads and creates `~/.pi/agent/mcp-auth.json`.
- OpenWaggle disposes the Pi `AgentSession` after every Run (`withPiSession`), so `createTransport` returns a lease on the Session's pooled connection (`adapters/mcp/pool/`): repeated `initialize` is answered from the cached handshake, `initialized` is forwarded once, request ids and progress tokens are renumbered upstream (every Pi client restarts at id 1), and closing a lease detaches instead of closing. Expired HTTP sessions and auth-required errors discard the upstream so Pi's own reconnect opens a fresh one. Pi only captures stderr when the transport `instanceof StdioTransport`, so the pool appends the stderr tail to start and handshake errors itself.
- The leak this fixes: connections used to close only after settings/auth/vault changes, and a measured install held 9 copies of four `npx` servers (~700–900 MB per Session that ran a turn). Now a Session's connections close 5 minutes after its last Run (`beginRun`/`endRun` around `runPiAgentKernel`), on archive/delete through `runSessionRemovalHooks` in `withSessionDesktopRemoval` (covers Hive Worker auto-archive), at Host stop (`disposeAppRuntime`), and under an LRU cap of warm Sessions (now 3; Sessions with a Run are never evicted). pi-mcp's `StdioTransport` spawns servers in their own process group and kills the group (`taskkill /T` on Windows), which stops `npx`/`uvx` wrappers with their server; `pi-mcp-transport-layer.integration.test.ts` proves it with a real wrapper process.
- MCP memory and start cost (v1 performance, October 2026): besides the warm cap of 3 Sessions above, a Worker whose Delegation is `ready_for_review`/`accepted`/`cancelled` closes its servers 60 s after its Run (`mcp-settled-worker-idle-policy.ts`). Each pooled connection records its server's `initialize` + `tools/list` pages in `<userData>/mcp/tool-catalogs/` (key: identity, connection fingerprint with `cwd: ''`, project); a catalog < 24 h old answers a lease's `initialize`/`tools/list`/`ping` at once, and a stdio server starts only on the first other request (`mcp-connection-lazy-start.ts`), replaying the client's `initialize`, then re-lists and sends `notifications/tools/list_changed` if tools differ. A failed lazy start forgets the catalog. URL servers stay eager. Servers without a `description` get the first line of their cached `instructions` in `loadConfig`, so `<mcp_servers>` is byte-stable. npx/npm exec/pnpm dlx/bunx launches spawn the bin the runner resolved (background probe `--package=<spec> -- node -e …` printing PATH and execPath; `<userData>/mcp/package-runner-bins.json`, 24 h for tags, 7 d pinned; Check connection passes `refreshPackage`). Measured: wrapper tree 274 MB and 1.3–3.3 s to `initialize` vs direct 93–169 MB and 0.34–0.40 s (chrome-devtools-mcp); 6 Sessions × 2 npx servers 31 processes / ~2.0 GB → 7 / 0.47–0.73 GB when 2 Sessions call a server.
- R5 review fixes to the above (October 2026): package-runner resolutions are keyed by a `file:` spec's resolved folder and the content of the nearest `.npmrc` / `pnpm-workspace.yaml` too (one project's `file:./x` spawned another project's package); the probe never goes through Node's `shell: true` (on Windows `cmd.exe` split its source at `||`, so the bypass never worked there): it runs `cmd.exe /d /s /c` with one cross-spawn-style escaped line (`windowsCommandLine`), and a failed probe is not retried for a day unless Check connection refreshes it. A probe that times out has its whole tree killed (`createExecProbe`: its own process group on POSIX, `taskkill /T /F` on Windows); `execFile`'s timeout killed only `cmd.exe`/`npx`, and their `node` and install ran on (V1 P3-1). `node <bin>` is used only for a plain `node` shebang; other shebangs run the file itself on POSIX and keep the runner on Windows, and `bunx --bun` keeps the runner. A stdio server's `env` values (`!command`) are resolved once per spawn (`openPreparedPiMcpUpstream`). A connection serving its catalog is not `isReady` and is left out of `connectionStates`, and served `tools/list` pages carry `{ served: true }` so they record App tools but never the status registry (a failed check stays failed). Check connection, sign-in/out, and turning a server off forget its catalog (its own handshake lacks OpenWaggle's client capabilities, so its pages are not written instead). A lazy start OpenWaggle interrupts by closing (`closedByOwner`) keeps the catalog. Catalog and bins files are 0600 in 0700 directories, the bins file is renamed into place, and a Host's first catalog write prunes catalogs past the TTL. The catalog-fill and runner-probe waits each take a third of the server's Pi `timeout` (60 s default), capped at 30 s. The first-request unknown-App wait stops for a server whose leased connection failed (`pool.connectionFailed`) or that a check or listing saw `failed`/`needs-sign-in` since the Run started; an older identity-wide state is not used to skip it (it has no project, and may predate a fix: V2 P3-2). A file's `direct` stays `direct` in a Run where codemode cannot reach MCP tools (`mcpCodemodeReach`: a `codemode` tool with `reachesTools` registered, and `autoEnableCodemode` or already active), by maintainer decision; `openwaggle mcp add --exposure direct` stays refused (exit 2). `knownMcpAppTools` asks the project catalog before the identity-only status registry. The settled-Worker idle policy reads `SessionDelegationRepository.delegationStateOf`, injected at Host start.
- Embedding never runs in the Session Host (D3): `MultilingualE5SessionEmbeddingModel` sends batches to a child (`session-embedding-process.ts`: Electron as Node, `getSafeChildEnv`, `serialization: 'advanced'`, ORT `intraOpNumThreads: 2`) that exits 60 s after its last batch or on `dispose()`. On disconnect the child kills itself with SIGKILL, not `process.exit()`: a normal exit after the model loads runs onnxruntime-node's static `Ort::Env` destructor, which throws (`mutex lock failed`) and SIGABRTs, and macOS showed an "OpenWaggle quit unexpectedly" dialog every time the child went idle (beta.29). A worker thread does not work: after `pipeline.dispose()` and `worker.terminate()` the process RSS stayed at ~730 MB. In-process inference held 1.2 GB and stalled the loop ~950 ms per batch of 32 on the large profile; the child keeps Host RSS ~95 MB with p99 loop delay 12–21 ms. Discovery re-embeds a Session only once its queue row is 15 s old (`settleMs`).
- Host background work (A-F9, decision 4): `hostBackgroundGate` (`application/host-background-gate.ts`) is open until `installHostIdleSlim()` enables it at Host start; then discovery, semantic/transcript projection and term repair wait until a client or a Run (`SessionHostLiveness.acquire('client' | 'run')`, `clientAttached` / `runStarted`) has been present 5 s, and pause while none is, so a headless Host still indexes Sessions a CLI Hive or Scheduled task creates. 2 s after the last one leaves, and at most once per `HOST_IDLE_SLIM_MIN_INTERVAL_MS` (60 s, since each short CLI command attaches and leaves), slim hooks close idle Sessions' MCP connections (`closeIdleSessions`, Runs keep theirs), end the embedding child, and run a V8 gc (`--expose-gc` via `v8.setFlagsFromString` + `vm.runInNewContext('gc')`). The embedding child must answer within 120 s of its last answer while batches wait (`SESSION_EMBEDDING_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`), or it is killed and the batches fail.
- Background work also gives way to clients (W12): every non-long-poll Local Session command (`LocalSessionInflightCommands.track` tells the gate) holds `whenForegroundQuiet()` until it ends plus 50 ms, for at most 1 s per wait; a command older than 2 s stops holding. Background passes yield with `yieldToHostForeground` / `awaitHostBackgroundTurn` (`application/host-background-turn.ts`), never inside a transaction. An event-loop yield alone was not enough: better-sqlite3 runs on the Host thread and Effect SQL serializes on the connection, so each statement of a request waited behind a ~30 ms repair slice and a Session open took 1.1–2.2 s while the term repair ran. Perf warm templates are warmed without a client, so the repair runs in every measured launch 5 s after attach.
- Configuration is five files folded in order (`~/.pi/agent/mcp.json`, `~/.openwaggle/mcp.json`, `.mcp.json`, `.pi/mcp.json`, `.openwaggle/mcp.json`) by `domain/mcp/config/mcp-config-merge.ts`, a port of Pi's `loadMcpConfig` that applies Pi's project-override rule to every file after the first. Pi's `loadConfig` is synchronous, so the config (vault references resolved) is loaded before the Pi session is created. Pi rejects `type: "sse"` and `ws:` URLs; OpenWaggle accepts them, gives Pi a plain URL entry, and keeps the transport kind in a side map for `createTransport`.
- Sign-ins: `McpOAuthCredentialStore` over `mcp-oauth-state-backend.ts`, a synchronous `AuthStorageBackend` (Pi's contract is sync) that merges Pi's `mcp-auth.json` (read-only) under OpenWaggle's encrypted `<userData>/mcp/oauth-states.json` (per build: dev and release have different safeStorage keys; a decrypt failure leaves the file untouched and refuses writes, only a parse failure sets it aside; the old shared `~/.openwaggle/mcp/oauth-states.json` and `vault.json` are copied once by the build that can decrypt them). Removing a Pi-only key writes a tombstone with the hash of Pi's value, so a later `pi mcp login` shows through. A refresh of a Pi-file token in OpenWaggle stores the rotated tokens in OpenWaggle only; a server that rotates refresh tokens then invalidates the copy Pi's CLI still holds.
- Runtime MCP exposure (ADR 0051 amendment): OpenWaggle runs a file's `direct` (server or `toolExposure`) as `codemode` in `loadMcpRuntimeConfig` (`withOpenWaggleRuntimeExposure`); `merged` and the files keep `direct` for `pi`. MCP App tools stay declared: known ones (Session `mcp-app-registry`, else status registry by identity) become exact `toolExposure` `direct` in Pi's `loadConfig`, which is also the only thing that now makes Pi's 1.5 s startup wait apply; newly listed ones are re-registered `direct` by `withOpenWaggleMcpExposure`'s `registerTool` proxy, which relies on Pi's MCP tool `namespace.aliases[0]` = server and label `<server>/<tool>` (`pi-mcp-runtime-exposure.integration.test.ts` fails if Pi changes that). Registered (extension) servers only get the stable `direct` rewrite in `getMcpServers`: Pi reconnects a registered server whose config JSON changes. Settings offers codemode/hidden (+ `deferred` only while a file sets it).
- Approval: the companion extension's `tool_call` handler raises an authorization for every `mcp__*` call, including codemode children (`parentToolCallId`), keyed `{ requesterId: mcpServerIdentity(name, command|url), resource: Pi tool name }`. Pi's resource tools are not gated (read-only).
- Sampling and elicitation are answered in the pool (`handleServerRequest`) through the interactions of the Session's live Pi session (attached at `session_start`); between Runs they are refused. MCP Apps: Pi strips `_meta` from tools and results, so the pool's `observeResult` records `tools/list` `_meta.ui.resourceUri` per Session and server, the companion `tool_result` handler adds `details.mcpApp`, and `mcp:read-resource`/`mcp:call-app-tool` go over the Session's pooled connection (opened on demand with the current config). The App descriptor's `serverInstanceId` is the server name and `serverConfigHash` the server identity. The frame's CSP allows the origins the resource declares in `_meta.ui.csp` (ADR 0053; before it the grant list was always empty, so CDN-loaded Apps such as Atlassian's Jira App rendered as an empty 320 px box). Most real Apps are a stub HTML that loads a module script from the server owner's CDN; the sandboxed frame has an opaque origin, which works because those CDNs send `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. A `srcdoc` App frame inherits the renderer's page CSP (no network scripts), so App documents are served from `openwaggle-mcp-app://frame-<uuid>/document` (`mcp-app-frame-protocol.ts`, registered over `mcp:register-app-frame`) with their own CSP header; the frame is `allow-scripts allow-same-origin` on that unique origin (Atlassian's App reads `document.cookie` and IndexedDB and throws in an opaque origin), and the origin's storage is cleared on unregister. OpenWaggle's theme is the `data-theme` attribute, not a `dark` class; Apps were told `light` until `use-mcp-app-frame-layout.ts` read it. The frame starts 1 px high (not 0: Chromium throttles rAF in zero-area cross-origin frames, and the ext-apps `autoResize` measures in rAF), takes the App's reported height, and shows a note after 15 s without a handshake.
- Opening Settings > MCP must not start servers. It reads `mcp:get-server-statuses` (warm pooled connection by server identity, else the in-memory last check per identity, else not checked) and only **Check connection** calls `mcp:inspect-server`. The pool's `connectionStates(identity)` is read-only: no request, no recency change, no connection handed to another Session; tools come from the `tools/list` results `observeResult` saw. Before this, every open spawned every enabled stdio server once (useQueries over `inspect-server`).
- `pnpm test:mcp:conformance` drives pi-mcp's `McpClient` through a pool lease and OpenWaggle's hardened fetch. The script is `.mts` because pi-mcp and pi-coding-agent are ESM-only and tsx loads `.ts` scripts as CommonJS; it must not import modules that import `@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent`.
- The hardened HTTP fetch (`mcp-server-fetch.ts`) pins DNS per request and bounds bodies and SSE events at pi-mcp's 16 MiB; the configured server may be plain HTTP or private (Pi parity), other hosts (OAuth endpoints) must be public HTTPS, and redirects may not change host. Pi's MCP tool registration only forces an object root (`toParameters`), so the transport layer's `adaptResult` hook rewrites every `tools/list` result with `providerToolParameters` (`adapters/pi/mcp/mcp-tool-schema-repair.ts`) before Pi's client reads it; `pi-mcp-tool-schemas.integration.test.ts` pushes a root-`anyOf` server tool through Pi's real Bedrock and OpenAI request builders.
- MCP Registry install (`adapters/mcp/registry/`) writes a plain Pi entry through `McpConfigurationService.addServer`. Pi resolves `$NAME`, `${NAME}`, and a leading `!command` in every `env` and `headers` string when it creates the transport, so any value taken from the Registry or an MCPB manifest is escaped (`$` → `$$`, leading `!` → `$!`); otherwise a remote server's declared header default could run a local command. Registry secrets become `{ "secret": NAME }` vault references, which is why installs only write OpenWaggle's files. The public Registry (`/v0.1/servers?search=`) took 0.5–13.5 s per search when measured, so requests get 30 s.
- Datadog's `datadog_mcp_cli` 0.2.8 (stdio proxy to `mcp.datadoghq.<site>/api/unstable/mcp-server/mcp`) can get stuck (trigger not pinned down: failures followed a 401 retry or a reconnect after the server's ~60 s idle GOAWAY, but a standalone probe with OpenWaggle's `initialize` and 5–120 s gaps never reproduced it): a request comes back `HTTP 400 Accept must contain both 'application/json' and 'text/event-stream'`, and it answers "Internal error" (-32603) to every request for the rest of the process's life. The proxy's own log is `~/Library/Logs/Datadog/datadog_mcp_cli.log.<date>`. Because the Session pool keeps the process warm across Runs, every Run warned "datadog: failed: Internal error". `McpUpstreamReplacement` now replaces a process whose listing fails with an internal error and sends the listing again (once a minute at most, only when nothing else is open; tool calls are never resent). `pi-mcp-stuck-server.integration.test.ts` reproduces it with a real child process. Datadog also deprecates that endpoint in its `initialize` instructions; `--endpoint-path v1/mcp` works with the same proxy.
- Never serialize MCP binary payloads into model-facing tool text. Base64 tokenizes at ~1.6 characters per token, so one Chrome DevTools screenshot (433,816 characters) added ~271k tokens and overflowed a 458,752-token window in a single tool step; Pi's chars/4 estimate predicted only ~108k, so the pre-sampling threshold could not catch it. Pi's MCP extension now converts results itself (`convertMcpResult`): images become native image content, other binary resources are saved to temp files and named by path, and text over 20 KB is cut in the middle with the full text saved. `src/shared/utils/mcp-binary-payload.ts` remains only for MCP App draft text; a change that puts raw MCP content into model-facing text must keep this rule.

## Electron Runtime Memory

- Hidden QA with a scratch `HOME` (to keep MCP or Pi files away from the real ones) hangs the Session Host on macOS without Chromium's `--use-mock-keychain`: `safeStorage` looks for the login keychain under `$HOME/Library/Keychains`, finds none, and blocks the main thread in a synchronous `AuthorizationCopyRights` XPC call, so every Host UI request times out ("Timed out waiting for the Local Session Host") while the process sits at 0% CPU. `start-electron-qa.ts` now sets `HOME` and `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` to `<profile>/qa-home` and always passes `--use-mock-keychain` (plus `--password-store=basic` on Linux), which the GUI forwards to the Host, so no Keychains symlink is needed (#358). Pick a free `OPENWAGGLE_QA_CDP_PORT` when 9223 is taken. `sample <pid>` shows the stack. `buildSafeElectronEnvironment` applies its overrides after the inherited allowlist, and forwards `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR`; before #358 an inherited `HOME` silently replaced a launcher's override.

Load `.agents/skills/electron-runtime/SKILL.md` for details.

- Electron 43 honours `--disk-cache-size` for every session, partitions included (`configureAppStoragePaths` appends it). It does not bound the V8 `Code Cache`; `chromium-disk-cache.ts` clears that, and an HTTP cache filled before the cap, by size: on each preview use (rate-limited, no timer, since preview tests count timers) and on a timer for the GUI's default session. The GUI and the Host share `session-data`, so only the GUI touches the default session's caches.
- Native addons have separate Node and Electron ABI targets. Rebuild with the repo scripts before blaming app code.
- xterm 6.0 stable lacks Kitty keyboard-protocol negotiation. The terminal pins the supply-chain-aged `@xterm/xterm@6.1.0-beta.303` exactly and enables `vtExtensions.kittyKeyboard`; a real-DOM test covers legacy-before-negotiation plus CSI-u press/release after `CSI > 3 u`. xterm calls custom key handlers on keydown and keyup in this line, so every app-owned/clipboard keydown must retain `event.code` and swallow its paired release or report-event-types clients receive an orphan keyup. Move back to stable only after those protocol tests and real-Electron performance/IME QA pass.
- Packaged apps may not inherit a shell PATH. Pi package/resource loading and Pi-run child processes that shell out to tools need an adapter-controlled npm-compatible PATH, including common user tool dirs such as `~/Library/pnpm` on macOS.
- `electron-builder` with pnpm can omit transitive runtime modules unless explicit dependencies are present; `ms` is intentionally explicit for `electron-updater`.
- macOS `electron-updater` requires ZIP artifacts in GitHub release metadata; DMG-only mac releases can advertise an update but fail with "ZIP file not provided".
- Squirrel.Mac counts every process with the app's bundle id *and* bundle path as a running instance (`NSRunningApplication`, accessory/`UIElement` included): the detached Session Host and any `openwaggle` CLI process (`mcp serve`, `hive wait`) from `/Applications/OpenWaggle.app` block an install. ShipIt waits for the instances it sees at start, then re-checks after verifying the update and aborts with `SQRLInstallerErrorDomain -9 "App Still Running"` if any is there, which is exactly what reopening the old version during the install window does. Evidence lives in `~/Library/Caches/com.openwaggle.app.ShipIt/ShipIt_stderr.log` (local time) and `ShipItState.plist` (JSON despite its name; it survives a successful install). Every install path (Restart to update, `openwaggle update`, `install.sh`) therefore stops the Host first with a `purpose: 'update'` stop (ADR 0047). `install.sh` must run the installed CLI with `</dev/null` (under `curl … | bash` stdin is the rest of the script), so `host stop --update` asks on `/dev/tty` through `tty.ReadStream`; a plain `fs.createReadStream('/dev/tty')` keeps a pending threadpool read that can stop the process exiting. To check counting without a GUI, start an isolated Host from the installed bundle (`OPENWAGGLE_USER_DATA_DIR=<tmp> /Applications/OpenWaggle.app/Contents/MacOS/OpenWaggle hive list`, with `OPENWAGGLE_AGENT_RUN` unset) and enumerate `NSRunningApplication.runningApplicationsWithBundleIdentifier('com.openwaggle.app')` through `osascript -l JavaScript`.
- On macOS 26, `NSWorkspace.iconForFile` returns the icon as the Dock draws it, including the grey tile macOS puts behind icons that do not fill the rounded-square grid. Render a throwaway `.app` bundle holding a candidate `.icns` through it to check an icon without touching the Dock. The macOS icons come from `scripts/generate-macos-icons.ts` (Apple's 1024 grid: 824 px continuous-corner tile, 100 px margin); Windows/Linux keep the free-form mark.
- On Apple silicon, performance/package QA should use arm64 outputs, not Rosetta x64 output from a universal build folder.
- Electron Playwright E2E requires isolated user data and single-instance lock opt-out when another OpenWaggle instance is running.
- Agent-run Electron E2E and QA have a hard non-disruption contract: the app must not show or focus a BrowserWindow, open a native dialog, or launch an external application. Automated paths fail instead of exposing OS UI; visible Electron QA requires an explicit headed command.
- `pnpm dev:debug` is the non-disruptive CDP automation entry point. Visible CDP QA is opt-in through `pnpm dev:debug:headed`; `pnpm dev` remains the normal visible development command.
- Hidden `pnpm dev:debug` uses an ephemeral user-data directory and disables the single-instance lock. It must not focus an existing OpenWaggle process or read and write the normal development profile.
- Hidden agent QA owns CDP port 9223; port 9222 remains available for visible/manual debugging. The hidden launcher preflights 9223 and fails before launching Electron if another process owns it.
- Hidden QA also requires a per-run identity in the renderer URL before accepting a CDP page. Port preflight alone is racy and must never be treated as proof that the connected process belongs to the launcher.
- The non-disruption contract is enforced inside the main process, not only by launch scripts. OS-visible Electron actions go through an automation policy that fails closed, and repository standards reject new unguarded window-show/focus, native-dialog, and external-application call sites.
- Trusted-main and discovered Pi runtime extensions are not loaded during non-disruptive automation. Electron exposes its window constructors as non-configurable module properties, so dynamically imported extension code cannot be brought inside the repository's static hidden-window construction boundary. Pi skills, prompts, themes, context, models, and auth metadata still load. Packaged Hive QA may explicitly retain host-supplied first-party inline factories so the native `hive` tool is exercised while discovered global/project extensions remain disabled.
- Agent-run headed Electron QA requires the maintainer's explicit approval for that exact run. An agent cannot infer permission from a task needing native-UI coverage or from approval granted to an earlier run.
- `pnpm dev:debug` is a managed hidden-QA launcher. It owns the Electron child process, exclusive port-9223 lease, ephemeral profile, log forwarding, signal handling, stale-dead-process metadata recovery, and profile cleanup. Stopping the process group does not stop the Session Host, which is detached (its own session/process group): beta.25 QA saw `session-host-internal` orphaned with PPID 1, holding the deleted profile's SQLite files until its idle timer. `completeElectronQaShutdown` therefore ends with `stopElectronQaSessionHost`, which runs `shutdownSessionHostForQa` (drain through the version-handoff path, then hold ownership while `prepareQaProfileRemoval` clears the profile) after the Electron group has stopped; while Electron lives it would start a new Host. Under automation, servers that project MCP files define do not connect (`connectsProjectMcpServers`, `mcp-automation-policy.ts`): the repository's `.mcp.json` made the QA Host run `npx -y chrome-devtools-mcp@latest` against its own CDP port. `OPENWAGGLE_AUTOMATION_PROJECT_MCP=1` opts in; project overrides of global servers still apply.
- Managed launchers serialize lease recovery and acquisition, quarantine stale leases before deletion, pass only an allowlisted child environment, and always continue through process-tree cleanup when screenshot capture fails. Windows cleanup uses `taskkill /T /F`; POSIX cleanup signals the detached process group.
- The hidden-QA profile registers the current worktree as its selected project and starts with no fabricated sessions. Feature-specific tests or QA scripts add deterministic fixtures explicitly.
- Every scripted Electron launch is non-disruptive, including E2E, CDP QA, startup measurement, and website screenshot capture. The only visible paths are ordinary `pnpm dev` and explicitly headed commands; agent use of any headed path still requires exact-run approval.
