The snapshot remembers the message ids it held when the Run settled (`settledMessageIds`) across route writes; hydration and the reconnect merge leave those rows to the persisted transcript only once it clearly holds that Run (each settled user row persisted under its id or at the same `sessionNodeCreatedOrder`), and otherwise keep them, so a stale detail never loses messages. Without this, a snapshot left by a route that rendered the Run kept its answers under stream ids, which never match the persisted Pi entry ids, and reopening showed them twice. A seeded snapshot holds only its Run's messages, so hydration appends them after the persisted history (`placeSeededRunMessages`): no text reconciliation, dedupe by id or user Session log order, compaction anchors and summary counts shifted past the history. Text reconciliation of user rows never matches rows at different `sessionNodeCreatedOrder`, because live message ids differ from Session node ids. After a non-continues refresh the snapshot is kept only if a Run that started during the refresh seeded it (`withoutSettledRunRenderSnapshot`); archive and delete clear it too. `applyAgentTransportEvent` returns its input for events that change no message; compare by reference instead of listing event types. The chat route reuses one `useAgentChat` across Sessions, so `backgroundStreamingRef` alone can still be the previous Session's while the next Session's detail loads; background events write a route transcript only when `backgroundReconnectSessionIdRef` names the event's Session, and a Session change with no detail yet resets the background state, or a running Session's events would overwrite another Worker's run-start seed. The post-refresh clear deletes only a snapshot still marked as holding the settled Run, and settled rows are judged one at a time (an unsaved optimistic user row stays). `mergeBackgroundReconnectMessages` places a message only the cached transcript holds after the message it followed, or before the first shared message, in one pass. A renderer reload mid-Run still loses the earlier answers until the Run ends.
- Agent-loop cards (interactions, notices, extension custom messages) are placed in the transcript, not appended below the newest answer (`lib/agent-loop-card-placement.ts`). A persisted card's audit chain hangs from the end of the Run that recorded it (`appendDurableAgentLoopEvents`); `readAgentLoopEventsFromWorkspace` returns that anchor message per event key, which bounds the card to its Run, and within it the card follows the last message whose `createdAt` precedes the event's Host timestamp. Live cards use the same rule from the newest message. A card never splits a tool call from its nested results and is deferred past a Waggle turn it falls inside. Audit nodes' `created_order` is renumbered on every snapshot, so never place cards by it.
- Rows are rebuilt for the whole branch on every streamed delta. `chatRowKeys` through `matchBy` cost about 15 ms per delta at 20,000 rows; message rows now take a fast path, keys are cached per row object, and `createMessageRow` / nested tool-result rows keep their identity while their inputs are unchanged (`lib/chat-message-row-model.ts`), so the key cache hits. Never mutate a row object. Per-delta resolve + rows + keys measured about 24 ms before and 11 ms after at 20,000 messages (6.3 ms to 2.6 ms at 6,000), in Node.
- Transcript order is guarded by the transcript-order harness (`features/chat/hooks/__tests__/transcript-order.*`): a Host model holds the Pi log, streams realistic transport events (tool turns, steers, promotions, queued Follow-ups with `continues`, compaction), injects Session switches, stalls with resync, slow detail reads, renderer reloads and Host restarts, and drives the real chat hooks, checking after every step that shown rows follow Pi order with no duplicates and pending steers last. Add a named scenario for every placement bug. On main, a reconnect mid-Run put retained steers right after a persisted history that lacked the Run's earlier answers, and the merge appended cached-only answers after them, so a steer jumped above the Run's earlier answers. A reconnect now places each retained user message after the answer it followed (`afterAssistantMessageId`, after earlier steers on that answer), or, when that answer is not shown, above the first answer the renderer received later; answers only the current transcript holds wait behind `earlierMessageIds` (persisted ids plus the Run's pre-first-answer user messages). Pending steer previews always render last (no captured baseline length). A Host resync forces rehydration, in place for a foreground Run. Hydration depends on the selected Session's activity boolean, not the stable `hasActiveRun` function. The Electron stream-buffer replica starts each new runId empty, like the Host's; keeping the settled Run's answer across `continues` showed it twice above the queued Follow-up. Review rounds added: answers are dated by Host event time, never the renderer clock (delivery lag otherwise moved steers above earlier answers); `BackgroundRunSnapshot.runId` is set where the Host starts the Run and carried through snapshot, decode and restore, so a restored replica still recognises another Run; a foreground reconnect merges only the buffer's rows, and a background one reads buffer, detail, buffer and keeps the result only when both buffer reads name the same Run; dropping streamed answers that the detail saved under Pi ids matches by content, one saved copy each, and only among answers saved since the reconnected Run started, because an unscoped match dropped a Run's "ok" when the previous Run had also answered "ok". The harness model repeats answer and prompt texts on purpose and fails on any received message missing while no Host read is held.
- Renderer Run lifecycle (`features/chat/lib/run-ids.ts`, `useBackgroundRunMonitor`): an `agent_start` under a runId the renderer already holds is the same Run (auto-retry or a Pi continuation after an `agent_end` without `willRetry`); `agent_end` never ends a Run, it only gives a snapshot's unnamed Run (`remote-snapshot:` start, reload restore) its real id; a Run ends only at its settlement. An agent-requested Waggle streams as `waggle-of-<X>` and settles as X (`settlingRunId`). A settlement can reach the renderer after the next Run started (writer successor, slow bridge), so the monitor keeps each Session's ordered started runIds and treats a settlement as an earlier Run's only when that Run is listed and a named Run started after it; it then only notes the snapshot and refetches. A settlement naming a Run that never started (failure before start) settles normally. Steer previews survive `agent_end` and clear at settlement or the next named start, because the Host returns untaken steers to the queue only at settlement. Each rule above came from a review round that found a lost, doubled or stuck transcript; the transcript-order model produces these Host sequences (retry, continuation, requested Waggle, failure before start, late settlement, settlement clearing the Host buffer before `run-completed` arrives) and checks liveness as well as order.
- First-message sends must bind to the concrete newly created session before async send begins; do not enqueue by current active session after users can switch projects.
- First-send worktree recovery must retain the exact submitted payload, Waggle config, and model until terminal transcript reconciliation proves delivery. Session Host command acceptance only means the supervised Run was scheduled; worktree birth and Pi execution can still fail asynchronously. Retry and Work locally replay that retained turn once; reading current composer preferences during recovery changes the user's request.
- Session tree/header refreshes for background sessions must not overwrite the active session tree/header.
- Session-native transcript rendering reads from the active `SessionWorkspace.transcriptPath`; preserve live tails only at active branch head.
- A completed run can refresh `SessionDetail` before `SessionWorkspace`. If those snapshots have no shared message ids, append the replacement detail only when its `updatedAt` proves the active-head workspace is stale; never merge a disjoint current selected-branch workspace. If a replacement Pi snapshot no longer contains the previous main head, branch derivation must adopt the new active head as the sole main branch instead of creating duplicate main/non-main branches at one head.
- TanStack Router uses hash history in Electron QA; navigate to `http://localhost:5173/#/<route>`.
- TanStack Hotkeys same-target callbacks do not stop each other via `event.stopPropagation`; independent overlays need explicit topmost ordering.
- A transient Session Summary must join the shared Escape stack and defer both Escape and pointer dismissal to foreground native dialogs. Image pinch zoom needs a canvas-local non-passive wheel listener because React delegates wheel events passively. Summary popovers must escape section clipping without losing their DOM ownership or viewport bounds; visible text alone does not prove a lower menu action can receive a click.
- Composer slash selection is owned by Lexical: keep focus in the editor, derive the active `/query` from its collapsed selection, replace or consume only that token, and serialize skill decorator nodes as `/skill-id`. Do not route `/` through a second-input global palette.
- Waggle presets in the desktop composer are one-shot invocation metadata, not idle global mode state. A standard agent hands off through the terminating `waggle_invoke` Pi tool, and the main handler chains Waggle only after the standard result is durable.
- A queued composer submission preserves its complete one-shot Waggle invocation in the durable Session Control intent and replays it through the Waggle executor under the Session's current authorization ceiling, Agent-definition allowlists, identity, capabilities, and pending upstream/downstream context. Never downgrade a queued Waggle submission into a classic Run.
- Follow-up acknowledgement is Host acceptance, not optimistic local dispatch. Pending draft ownership must survive composer unmounts and navigation; deduplicate by scoped draft identity and clear the currently mounted editor only after acknowledgement, without erasing a newer draft.
- A Session Control `message` starts a Run when the Session is idle and its queue will not deliver on its own (empty, paused, or head needing attention); otherwise it appends a Follow-up and `agent:send-message` reports `queued` (the renderer then hands back the displaced foreground state, `useAgentChat.foreground-run.ts`). Before this, a failed Run's paused queue swallowed every later message and the renderer waited forever for a `run-completed` (Stop and "Thinking" over an idle Session). A failed/interrupted Run pauses only Follow-ups accepted up to its terminal `agent_end` (`terminalEventAt`, Session Control clock, vs `intent.acceptedAt`); one accepted after it is an explicit retry: the first such item starts at settlement, and the queue pauses only if earlier items remain (`planRunSettlement`). The pause reason (`run-failed`, `run-interrupted`, `run-timed-out`, `parent-limit`, `host-lost`, `profile-revoked`, `requested`) is stored in `session_control_states.queue_pause_reason` (migration 62; set on running->paused, cleared on resume) and returned by `queue-list`/`status`.
- Follow-up edits (ADR 0044, Local Session revision 21, capability `sessions:follow-up-edit-v1`) are three journaled Session Control ops: `queue-edit-begin` -> `follow-up-edit-held` {holdId, queueRevision, leaseExpiresAt}, `queue-edit-save` {holdId, expectedQueueRevision, input: text/attachmentIds/waggle?/visualizationContext?}, `queue-edit-cancel` {holdId}. Only `gui:local-user` without a profile may edit (`follow_up_edit_requires_desktop_user`), and only a Follow-up that caller queued itself, not one it adopted (`canEditFollowUp`: `callerId` matches and there is no `authorCallerId`): attachments resolve under the author (`authorCallerId ?? callerId`). Timeout, `acceptedAt`, and idempotency key never change; a Follow-up has no thinking level or authorization override. A save names the revision its edit began at (`editHold.baseQueueRevision`), not the queue's current one: while held only the holder changes the item, and anything else that changes it ends the hold (withdrawal, delivery, adoption by another caller), so reorders and other items never refuse a save. Cancelling a gone hold is an accepted no-op.
- Local Session revision 22 (`events:run-snapshot-content-v1`): active Run snapshot text and reasoning parts carry `contentIndex` so the Host buffer splits an answer the way the live view does, and reconnect merges match parts by identity (tool id, stepId) instead of position. Parts are exact-decoded, so revision-21 clients are refused at the handshake rather than failing on a snapshot. `degraded.messageCutShort` says whether the caps cut the streaming message, because `omittedBytes` is Run-wide; the run-level flag made every later answer look degraded.
- Every field the Host buffer puts on an active Run snapshot part must be in the client's exact decoder (`messagePartSchema` in `local-session-snapshot-parts.ts`, used by `local-session-active-run-snapshots.ts`). Pi 1.2.0 (#303) made the buffer record a codemode call's nested calls as `nestedCalls` on its tool-call part (`ToolCallResult` allows it too), and the decoder did not list it. The first active Run that used codemode made `decodeActiveRunSnapshots` throw, which failed the whole renderer-bridge subscription; it retried every 30 s into the same snapshot, so no Session received live events (transcripts on "Thinking...", rows on "Connecting") while Runs finished in the Host. Log signature: `[session-host/renderer-bridge] Remote Session Host renderer connection is degraded` with a schema path ending in `["nestedCalls"] is unexpected`. Since #308:
  - Type tests (`local-session-snapshot-parts.unit.test.ts`) hold the keys of each `messagePartSchema` member and of the attachment, tool-call and tool-result records to `MessagePart`'s, and `MessagePart` must extend the decoded type so a widened literal fails too; `@shared/schemas/nested-tool-calls` is checked two-way against `NestedToolCalls`, and session-details hydration reuses it. The same audit found `AttachmentRecord.browserPreview` missing from the snapshot decoder.
  - Only a snapshot without a Run identity fails the decode, because the bridge reads a Session missing from the snapshots as a settled Run. An undecodable streaming part is left out, counted in `degraded.omittedBytes` and marks the message cut short; an earlier assistant message holding one is left out; undecodable user messages, activity events or worktree launch are left out. Each is logged by `session-host/run-snapshots` with sessionId, runId and the failing schema paths and issue tags only (`getParseIssuePaths`): Effect issue messages embed the failing value, which is transcript or tool content.
  - Live events had the same failure: a failed subscription resubscribes after the same cursor and the Host replays the same event. `decideSessionHostEvent` relays a `message_start` without a user message it cannot decode, skips any other undecodable event past its cursor (`onCursor`), and fails only on an event without a cursor. Logged by `session-host/event-client` with kind, sessionId, event type and sequence.
  - No Local Session revision bump for this kind of fix: the Host already sent the field under revision 24, the client was catching up. Bumping would only turn old clients' hang into a handshake refusal and make the fixed GUI refuse a still-running beta Host.
- Local Session revisions are assigned in merge order, not branch order. Title regeneration (ADR 0043) landed on main as revision 20 while the Follow-up edit and Session settings branch also used 20; the branch moved to 21 (`LOCAL_SESSION_REVISION_21_CAPABILITIES`, `HOST_UI_REVISION_21_REQUIRED_CHANNELS`). When syncing a branch that bumped `LOCAL_SESSION_CURRENT_REVISION`, keep main's number and renumber the branch's constants, capability list, channel list, the revision checks in both `local-session-command-revision.ts` and `local-session-client.ts`, and every `[N]` in `src/main/session-host/__tests__/*revision*` and the negotiation test (including the future-client `[N+2, N+1]` case).
- The v1 performance branch's bounded Session reads (`LOCAL_SESSION_SESSION_DATA_REVISION`, `host-ui:session-data-v1`, `HOST_UI_REVISION_29_REQUIRED_CHANNELS`/`_ARGUMENT_COUNTS`) were revision 28 on the branch and became 29 when main shipped Heartbeat efficiency as 28 in beta.28. Its migrations 80–83 already sorted after main's 75, so only the ledger identity list needed 75 inserted before them. Ledger-reading migration tests bound their `_migrations` query above (`id <= <their migration>`) so later migrations never change what they expect.
- A hold (`SessionControlFollowUp.editHold`) travels with reorder and stops delivery at the held item: settlement, the failed-Run retry (which then waits as `followUpQueue.deferredRetryAfter`), resume, promotion (`follow_up_edit_held`), and a message to an idle Session whose running queue has a held head (queued behind; a paused one still starts the message). Invariant: an idle Session never keeps a runnable queue. `deliverIdleQueueHead` runs after every accepted withdraw/reorder/resume/adopt/edit-save/edit-cancel (`sqlite-session-control-queue-delivery.ts`, needs `nextRunId` on `executeMutation`; the outcome becomes `started-run`) and every settlement; if the Host cannot admit that Run the queue pauses (`parent-limit`, or no reason for the Host ceiling: older binaries throw on unknown reasons, so never add one casually). Worker Delegation settlement is deferred only while the queue runs and its head is a pending held Follow-up (`waitsOnHeldNextFollowUp`); the completed Run is kept in TEMP `session_deferred_worker_settlements` (`sqlite-deferred-worker-settlement.ts`) and settled by `settleDeferredWorkerDelegationAfterQueueChange` after any accepted queue/edit change that leaves the Session idle and no longer waiting (lost on Host restart). It runs after the change committed, so it never fails the command (logged, stays deferred), and its Hive cleanup request runs only after the Worker's command serialization is released: an inline cleanup takes that non-reentrant serialization itself. `pauseStrandedFollowUps` leaves a queue with a held head running. `returnUndeliveredSteers` drops direct steers past `MAX_FOLLOW_UP_QUEUE_LISTED_ITEMS` so a reorder can always name every id; settlement logs each drop after commit by follow-up id, caller, and idempotency key (`returned-steer-drop-report.ts`), since its caller already holds a steered-Run receipt; an edit save is refused for bytes only when it grows a queue past the cap. Startup recovery already pauses every running non-empty queue as `host-lost`.
- Holds, deferred retries, and retained edit attachments live in connection-scoped SQLite TEMP tables (`sqlite-follow-up-edit-holds.ts`), overlaid by `loadSessionControlState` and rewritten by `persistSessionControlState`: transactional with the queue, no migration, gone on Host restart. Leases are counted in Host sweeps (`missed_sweeps`, expired at `FOLLOW_UP_EDIT_HOLD_LEASE_SWEEPS` = 6 sweeps of 5 s), never clock time: Windows' monotonic clock counts across sleep, a sweep timer does not. Sweeps run only in the Host process (`startSessionHostOwnedServices`), which is the only place holds exist. Exposed `leaseExpiresAt` is a wall-clock estimate (`estimatedLeaseExpiry`); attachment retention still uses `utils/monotonic-clock.ts`. Holds are renewed every 10 s and on `powerMonitor` resume by Electron main through the unjournaled `local-ui-v1` `renew-follow-up-edit-hold`; the window tracker (`ipc/follow-up-edit-hold-window-leases.ts`) cancels a window's holds on `destroyed`/`did-navigate`/`render-process-gone`, cancels a hold whose renewal reports it lost, and releases a begin whose page changed while it was in flight (`WindowPageGenerations`). Loads ignore expired holds and renewal refuses them; the Host sweep (`follow-up-edit-hold-expiry.ts`, 5 s) advances leases (`advanceLeases`) and returns expired holds without deleting them and sends the holder's cancel (key `follow-up-edit-hold-expired:<holdId>`), whose persist removes the row, so a failed cancel retries. A replayed begin whose hold died is answered `follow_up_edit_not_held` (`validateReplay`). A draining Host still admits `renew-follow-up-edit-hold` and `queue-edit-cancel`; withdraw/reorder/adopt/edit-save/edit-cancel take the Run lease optionally and, without it, apply with `queueDeliveryAdmitted: false` (no `nextRunId`). A re-adopted edit is bound to the adopting window through `session-control:adopt-follow-up-edit` (renew, then `track`), via `useSessionFollowUpQueue().adoptEdit`, before the composer opens it. Attachments a held intent or a desktop user's save names are kept from `cleanupUnreferenced` for an hour so a retried save or a lost edit queued as new can still bind them. Renderer API: `useSessionFollowUpQueue().beginEdit/resumeEdit/adoptEdit/saveEdit/cancelEdit` (`adoptEdit` re-binds an edit; `adopt(followUpId)` is the unrelated `queue-adopt`), `isLostFollowUpEdit`, `snapshot.waitingOnEdit` (the composer must `enqueue` an explicit Waggle while it holds: the explicit Waggle contract is a journaled run-now replacement with no queued outcome, so the Host cannot queue it behind the hold).
- Follow-up edit UI (composer feature): `useQueuedMessageEditStore` keeps this window's edit per Session (`beginning`/`editing`/`saving`/`cancelling`), bound to the draft `contextKey` it began in (drafts are per branch or node; the composer is in edit mode only while that draft is visible, otherwise it shows a cancel-able "edited in another branch" note), plus the `SessionFollowUpEdit` from `beginEdit` as `based`, passed unchanged to `saveEdit`/`cancelEdit`. Every step re-checks after its await that the store still holds the same holdId. The pre-edit draft is stashed in `scopedDrafts` under `follow-up-edit:session:<id>:stash` (inside the composer store so its attachments stay owned, session-scoped so Session deletion clears it), stashed before the edit replaces it and cleared only after it is written back. Queued attachments load as chips with `path: ''` and are protected by `retainHostReferencedAttachments` until the edit ends (`releaseHostReferencedAttachments`); the Host retains attachments a hold or save names, so a non-lost rejection keeps every chip for the retry and a lost edit kept as a new draft keeps the edit's attachments (still protected while their chips stay). `composer-activity-store` counts attachment preparations and queued-submission acknowledgements; Edit and Save wait for both (and for a branch-summary prompt). Voice stop-and-send in edit mode inserts the transcript and saves (`sendAfterInsert`). `useQueuedMessageEdit` is the only adapter to the hook's edit API; `useAdoptHeldQueuedMessageEdit` (mounted by `Composer` via `mode.queuedMessagesSessionId`, retried when the visible draft changes) is the single place that re-adopts a `heldByCurrentUser` hold on an `editable` item: it awaits `refresh()` and then `resumeEdit(id)`, which reads the query cache (not the render's `query.data`) so it sees the refetch, and carries `queueRevision` from `editHold.baseQueueRevision`. While `snapshot.waitingOnEdit`, the composer routes an explicit Waggle through `onEnqueue` even when idle (`useComposerSubmission({ enqueueWaggle })`), so it cannot overtake the held message. Dock moves are anchored to a neighbour id (`moveQueuedMessage`), send the full queue order with pending steering promotions locked in their slots (re-read on retry), replay once on `queue_revision_changed`, then focus the moved grip and announce the new position. Final review fixes: a lost save whose message the refreshed queue still shows as editable (`follow_up_edit_not_held`/`_hold_mismatch`) takes a fresh hold and keeps the user's draft (`continueEdit`); only a gone message becomes a new draft, and a still-queued uneditable one keeps its place with the text offered alongside. Dismissing the edited row keeps the edited text as a draft. `composer-activity-store` is keyed by draft context (preparation counted against the draft visible when it starts, submissions against the submitting draft); a blocked Edit stays clickable and toasts why. Escape: the editor defers to the shared `useEscapeHotkey` stack (`hasEnabledEscapeHandler`), cancels an unchanged edit at once and a changed one on a second press. `clearScopedDraftsForSession/ForBranch` end matching edits (`abandonQueuedMessageEdits`: cancel the hold, forget the edit, drop its stash). Host-referenced and submitted attachment marks are released when the chip leaves every draft. Move focus waits for the committed order (layout effect on the rendered ids) because React relocating the focused row blurs it in browsers; the move announcement is re-keyed by a nonce.
- Queue dock drag (#378): the grip is the drag source, so without `setDragImage` Chromium drags an image of the grip alone. `setQueuedMessageDragImage` (`composer/lib/queued-message-drag.ts`) hands it a copy of the whole row: same width, text clamped to 3 lines, framed on the dock background, row identity (`data-qa`, `data-follow-up-id`, ids) stripped, `aria-hidden` + `inert`, `position: fixed` at `left: -10000px` (still laid out, which `setDragImage` needs; no flash, no layout shift), removed on the next task (Chromium paints the image when `dragstart` returns). The source row gets `data-drag-source` (dimmed, dashed outline) only after the copy is taken. The list (`ul`), not each row, owns `dragover`/`dragleave`/`drop`: one drop line at a time (`data-drop-target` on a row, drawn as a `::before` in the 4px gap), kept while the pointer crosses the gap, cleared only when `dragleave.relatedTarget` is outside the list (Chromium sets it for drag events), and a drop lands where the line is (`dropAnchor` direction rule unchanged). `dragend` clears everything, which is also the Escape and drop-outside path. A dragged message that leaves the queue mid-drag (delivered, withdrawn) unmounts its grip, and so does a queue left with one message (`reorderable` needs two); Chromium's `dragend` on the detached node never reaches React's root listener, so a layout effect on the rendered order ends the drag in both cases. In hidden Electron QA the OS draws the drag image outside the page, so screenshots cannot show it: wrap `DataTransfer.prototype.setDragImage` and draw a copy of its element at its offset from the pointer. Drive drags with raw CDP (`Input.setInterceptDrags`, `Input.dispatchMouseEvent`, `Input.dispatchDragEvent` incl. `dragCancel`) and always finish each one with `drop` or `dragCancel` plus `mouseReleased`: an unfinished intercepted drag (including some Playwright `mouse` drags) leaves Chromium refusing new drags until reload. The app ships only the dark Appearance (plus dev-only `debug`, whose scale pushes the dock off screen), so light/high-contrast checks use token overrides and `forced-colors: active` emulation.
- The settle gap (Pi `agent_end` -> Host `run-settled`) measured 2.8 s in this repo, 2.6 s of it `captureTurnCheckpoint`: the snapshot index was built with `read-tree HEAD`, so `git add -A` re-hashed the whole worktree. Seeding the scratch index from a copy of the real index (keeping its mtime for racy-git) cut the gap to ~0.4 s. The rest is Pi post-run quiet time (~150 ms) and persistence. The renderer shows a `finishing` status in the gap (`run-finishing-store`: terminal `agent_end` until final `run-completed` or the next `agent_start`): no "Thinking", and a message sent then is enqueued as a Follow-up, which the retry rule starts right after settlement. Pi may still be working in that gap: it runs `agent_before_settle` handlers (a project extension's lint/type-check gate can take minutes) before `agent_settled`, which the listener now forwards as a transport event. Stop is offered there once the agent has not settled for `FINISHING_STOP_OFFER_DELAY_MS` (1 s, so it does not flash at the end of every Run), and not after `agent_settled`. Pi's `ctx.signal` was undefined in before-settle handlers (the loop's signal is gone) until OpenWaggle's patch gave them an abortable one; a handler that ignores it no longer holds a stopped Run: `runPiOperationWithinAbortGrace` settles it after 5 s once the agent loop has returned, without the post-run settlement wait (Pi stays `isStreaming` until the handler returns). Extension `ctx.ui.setStatus(key, text)` and `setWorkingMessage(text)` reach the renderer as live `extension_status` events (ANSI stripped, one line; not persisted, not stream-buffered, so a renderer reload loses them until the next update) and show on the Run's status row (`extension-status-store`, keyed per Run, cleared when it settles or another Run starts).
- A Run publishes one terminal `agent_end`: Pi's, classified at the adapter (`session-listener`, `run-lifecycle-failure`); the Host publishes one only when none went out (`endFailedRun`). A failure found after a clean `agent_end` (turn not saved) is reported on the settlement as `failureCode` (code only; `session-state-changed` needs just `sessions:discover`). `run-settled` and `follow-up-started` carry `runId` and `terminalStatus`; the GUI bridge emits `agent:run-completed` for both, the latter with `continues: true` (session-wide listeners ignore it). `agent:send-message` returns the started `runId`, and the renderer's foreground waiter only settles on its own Run's completion (earlier Runs' completions are forwarded to their sends; ones arriving before the id is known are held). Waggle sends still report no `runId` and take any completion.
- Workspace file UI is route-backed, but all indexing, root confinement, preview reads, optimistic-revision writes, and external-open resolution stay behind `WorkspaceFileService` in the main process.
- Edit rows (ADR 0050): Pi's edit result carries two diffs. `details.patch` is a standard unified patch (`--- path`/`+++ path`/`@@` hunks) that Pierre can render; `details.diff` is Pi's TUI display diff (`+12 code`, `-12 code`, no hunk headers) that Pierre cannot parse. Render `patch`, and fall back to `diff` as plain highlighted text only for edits recorded before Pi sent a patch. The old inline diff view fed `diff` to Pierre behind a fake file header, which is why it never rendered real hunks.
- Turn-settle folds (ADR 0034): interruption labeling must apply only to the run's last fold unit; older turns always render "Worked for Xs" from durable checkpoint durations. A reset phase timer (0 ms) means unknown duration, never a zero-second run. `turn_checkpoints` rows are recorded even with empty diffs so durations survive restarts for turns that changed no files.
- A Run whose tool calls are all known read-only (`isReadOnlyToolCall` in `agent-kernel/run-workspace-mutations.ts`) skips the worktree snapshot (v1 perf D11). Its checkpoint row carries the previous turn's `snapshot_ref`, anchored under its own turn ref, because `getLatestSnapshotRef` is the next turn's diff base: a null there diffs against HEAD and folds earlier uncommitted turns into the next diff. A new tool that can write files must not be added to the read-only lists. A session that loaded any extension from disk (not `<inline…>`/`builtin:`) with event handlers never skips: handlers such as a formatter on `agent_end` write without a tool call, and an `input` handler can turn the prompt into a command.
- Per-turn checkpoint rows store Pi-format `content_json` parts (`{type:'text',text}`, `{type:'tool-call',toolCall:{id,name,args,state}}`), not renderer Chat*Part format; seeding or mutating session rows with the wrong shape fails `sessions:get-workspace` projection.
- **React Compiler runs in the app build (`electron.vite.config.ts` -> `reactCompilerPreset()`) and, since this work, in the component test config too — but not in the node unit config, which renders nothing.** Any component that reads render data from a library-owned *mutable* instance can pass a suite that does not run the compiler and still render permanently stale in the real app: the compiler memoizes on referential identity, and the instance is mutated in place so its identity never changes. Hit for real with `@headless-tree` in the Changed-file navigator — `tree.getItems()` returned 262 items while zero rows reached the DOM. Fix is the scoped `'use no memo'` directive on the component that maps the mutable instance (an official compiler escape hatch, not a lint-ignore comment). See "The React Compiler now runs in component tests" below for the mechanism that now catches this class; for anything outside component tests, still treat a green suite as no evidence and verify in real Electron over CDP.
- Corollary: prefer libraries whose render input is a plain value over ones exposing a mutable instance, precisely because our tests cannot see the difference.
- A provider CLI can create a PR/MR remotely and still exit non-zero if its response is interrupted. Before surfacing create failure, resolve the exact head ref and adopt an existing request; after a successful create, retain the CLI's returned URL if the metadata lookup is transiently unavailable. This prevents duplicate requests on retry.
- Change-request creation follows the destination that actually received the push, including `pushurl`, remote branch, and repository owner. Require one compatible provider/authority/repository destination; GitHub fork creation uses `owner:branch`, recovery verifies owner/head/base, and browser fallback is withheld because it cannot verify the fork relationship. A bare local ref can target or adopt an unrelated same-named branch in the base repository.
- Hosted change-request identity accepts only network Git transports that preserve a meaningful provider authority (`http`, `https`, `ssh`, `git`, or SCP-like syntax). Never rewrite `file:` or an unknown remote-helper URL into a GitHub/GitLab web identity. A pinned push that uses command-scope `remote.<name>.pushurl` overrides must fail closed when the configured remote name contains `=`, because `git -c` would parse a different config key and silently stop pinning the approved destination.
- Main-process Git mutations are serialized by canonical checkout root, not the caller's opened subfolder. Worktree create/remove owns that same low-level boundary so IPC, first-send birth, and Session prune cannot race each other; linked worktrees remain distinct checkout identities.
- A successful commit or change request can still fail Session Output projection. The result contract exposes whether durable retry authorization was persisted; renderer actions must surface every projection failure and offer manual retry only for an authorized queued request, while retaining the remote request's browser URL.
- Session-resource run-completion invalidation is keyed by the event's Session id, not the currently opened Session. Background Sessions otherwise retain a fresh-looking cached catalog and omit newly captured resources when reopened.
- Extension resource contributions use the approved `openwaggle.resources` broker capability with explicit Session scope. Publish payloads accept only credential-free HTTPS links/images; the host derives actor, occurrence, canonical identity, and Session ownership. List results expose display metadata only—never locators, managed paths, canonical keys, or occurrence history—and invalidation events carry the affected Session id.
- Extension side panel icons (ADR 0043) are resolved only on the `extensions:list-contributions` Host UI path (`listExtensionContributionRegistryViewWithPanelIcons`); the broker and module-access paths keep using the plain registry view and never read icon files. A `{ svg }` icon is an *optional* content-hash file (`optionalFiles`, hashed with a missing/oversized marker), so the resolver can cache by content hash yet a missing icon never makes the package ineligible. The SVG is reduced by an allowlist parser in `src/main/domain/extension-panel-icon/` and painted only as a CSS mask (`ExtensionPanelIcon`), which the renderer CSP already allows through `img-src data:`.
- Session resources retain two distinct locator concepts: the original public locator for provenance/open/reveal and the host-managed path for safe rendering. Raster image status is established from stored bytes, not filenames or declared MIME; SVG remains an ordinary file rather than renderable active content.
- A remote image read refreshes its Session resource projection only after managed content materializes. Refreshing after a failed read bumps the resource revision and immediately repeats the same failed query; failures remain stable until the user explicitly retries.
- After Session Host cutover, Session-resource catalog, backfill, and managed-content reads are Host-owned: the GUI `AppDatabase` is client-isolated and cannot see durable Session nodes. Route resource operations through a revisioned Host UI contract, return only bounded validated image bytes for GUI-native clipboard/attachment/protocol actions, and never add a second database writer. Agent-emitted local Markdown images may be copied only from the Session workspace or dedicated `electron-qa-evidence`/`openwaggle-evidence` directories under the platform temp parents; never authorize the whole shared temp tree. Keep the transcript path as provenance while rendering only the managed copy.
- The desktop window's `AppDatabase` is client-isolated (`configureAppDatabaseAccess('client-isolated')` in `main/index.ts`), so any window-side `typedHandle` that reads `SessionProjectionRepository`, `SessionResourceRepository`, `SessionOutputRetryRepository`, or calls `SettingsService.update` silently sees an empty database or writes into one that disappears. That broke the Change request inspector ("This change request does not belong to the opened Session." on every open), merges, and every Session-scoped Commit/Push/Create PR from the Session Summary, whose Outputs were also lost (ADR 0048). Unit tests did not catch it because they run in owner mode. The fix pattern: a revisioned Host UI channel (`hostHandle`, or `invokeConfiguredHostUi` with a local fallback when the route is not configured) and, for window-side orchestration that relays a Host result, `relayingHandle` + `RelayedHostResult` instead of a cast. Settings reads from window-side services go through `invokeConfiguredHostUi('settings:get')`; source-control writes go through the Host's atomic `source-control:patch-settings`, as `makeSourceControlSettingsAccess` does (other settings use `settings:update`). Reproduce such bugs against `pnpm dev:debug` (set `OPENWAGGLE_QA_CDP_PORT` if 9223 is taken) by driving `window.api` over CDP, not with unit tests.
- Source-control provider resolution is `services/source-control/` (ADR 0048): never decide a provider from the hostname alone. A recognised host is kept as written so `Host github.com` → `ssh.github.com:443` SSH configs do not move it; `gh` and `glab` host lists are read from their config files (`hosts.yml`, glab `config.yml`) because `gh auth status`/`glab auth status` hit the network. Tests that assert exact git command lists must allow the resolver's read-only lookups (`isSourceControlResolverRead` in the stacked-gates harness).
- Source-control Settings are written only with `SourceControlSettingsAccess.patch` (per-entry set/null-remove, applied serially by the process that owns the database; the window sends `source-control:patch-settings` to the Host). A whole-record `settings:update` from the window raced with Host writes and dropped entries. `git ls-remote` patterns are globs matched against the end of a ref, so `refs/pull/` matched nothing; use `refs/pull/*/head`. Remote hosts are validated (`isSourceControlHostName`) before they reach a CLI argument, a settings key, or the terminal sign-in command; a crafted remote like `https://github;id;x/o/r` was otherwise typed into a shell. Writes through the gh account runner (`runWrite`) never retry as another account. Per-project source-control keys go through `projectSettingsKey` (the main checkout), or the agent and the Session Summary use different keys for the same project. Unit and integration configs point `GH_CONFIG_DIR`/`GLAB_CONFIG_DIR` at a missing directory so tests never read the developer's sign-ins.

### Streaming render cost (v1 performance, October 2026)

- **The build's React Compiler runs under Babel 8, where `isLVal()` is false for `AssignmentPattern`.** Unpatched, compiler 1.0.0 bailed out of every component or hook that destructures with a default (`function Row({ muted = false })`), and `panicThreshold: 'none'` shipped them uncompiled: 262 bailouts in 143 renderer files at beta.27. `patches/babel-plugin-react-compiler@1.0.0.patch` fixes it; `scripts/__tests__/react-compiler-coverage.unit.test.ts` fails if an upgrade regresses it. Research tools that run the compiler under Babel 7 do not see this class.
- `pnpm check:react-compiler` recompiles `features/chat` and `features/composer` and fails on any bailout. Common causes and fixes: `try/finally` or `try` without `catch` (use `withFinally`/`attempt` from `shared/lib/async-outcome.ts`, or move the body to a module function), conditionals inside `try`, reading `x.someRef` off a hook result during render (destructure it), passing an object of refs or a ref-writing closure to a function during render (create it in a `useState` initializer), a hook called as a value (`useStore.getState()` in the hook body), a default parameter that is an object literal, and hand-written `useCallback`/`useMemo` the compiler cannot preserve. A function that calls no hook is not compiled unless it says `'use memo'`.
- A streamed text or reasoning token only lengthens a part of the streaming message: it updates `messagesBySessionIdRef` and `live-stream-message-store`, and only that message's bubble re-renders (`useLiveStreamMessage`). Every other event commits the chat's message list, which carries all tokens since. So `useAgentChat().messages` can be behind the screen by the current text block; tests that check what the transcript shows use `withLiveStreamMessages`. `messagesBySessionIdRef` must never be synced back from state.
- The GUI main process coalesces `agent:event` deltas per window (`utils/agent-event-delivery.ts`): 33 ms for Sessions the window names through `agent:set-shown-sessions`, 250 ms for the rest. `broadcastToWindows` flushes pending deltas first, so no other channel overtakes an earlier token. Renderer code subscribes to `agent:event` only through `shared/lib/agent-event-hub.ts`.
- Streaming Markdown keeps its block split when the answer has link reference definitions: a one-line definition at a block start is collected (`definitions`), and each block and the live tail is parsed with the definitions whose label it mentions put in front (`withReferenceDefinitions`). Footnotes, definitions in quotes or lists, and multi-line ones still make the text one document. Lines in fenced code or raw HTML are never definitions; before, a `[x]: y` line in a Markdown/INI fence made every token re-parse the whole answer and lost the open-fence fast path (V1 P3-4).
- Measured at 4x CPU throttle (fake 125 tokens/s, 1,500 tokens, hidden automation window): p99 frame 116.7 → 16.1 ms, frames > 100 ms 122 → 2, renderer JS 14.6 → 2.7 s, `agent:event` deliveries 7,527 → 356.

### Renderer polish from the beta.25 QA (#356, October 2026)

- A Markdown image destination means what its author meant (`MarkdownImageOrigin` in `session-resource-extraction-types.ts`). Agents write local screenshots as plain absolute paths, but an `openwaggle_docs` topic writes its screenshots root-relative (`/screenshots/hive-sessions.png`), which are website pages; the installed docs bundle does not ship them. Backfill read them as local files, failed, and stored unavailable "Created by openwaggle_docs" outputs with no locator, whose Open called `shell:open-path` on `/screenshots/…`. `toolResultOutputGroups` now resolves an OpenWaggle topic's images against `OPENWAGGLE_WEBSITE_ORIGIN` (an image source the tool read) and ignores local image paths in Pi and extension topics (`details.source`). Every caller reads a tool group through `collectToolOutputGroupResources`, so live capture, backfill slots and display order agree. Migration 73 (after #366's 72) deletes the stored rows and resets those Sessions' backfill state.
- An unavailable resource offers neither Open nor Reveal (`resourceOriginalPath` returns a path only for an available one): its file could not be read, and a recorded path may not be a file at all.
- Electron rejects a failed `invoke` with `Error invoking remote method '<channel>': Error: <message>`. Show `ipcFailureMessage(cause, fallback)` (or `ipcErrorMessage`), never `cause.message`. `shell:open-path`, `shell:reveal-path` and `shell:open-external` check the path or URL first and fail with words for the user, logging the system's reason; a non-disruptive automation block keeps its own message.
- Chromium's accessibility tree (CDP `Accessibility.getFullAXTree`, so also the DevTools MCP snapshot) reports `valuemax 0` for every spinbutton without a maximum, a native `<input type="number" min="1">` included. `NumberStepper` already omits `aria-valuemax` without a maximum (`FormControls.component.test.tsx`); do not invent a maximum to hide the 0.
- Hide a region that can hold focus with `inert` alone. `aria-hidden` on an ancestor of the focused element makes Chromium warn "Blocked aria-hidden…", which the Scroll to bottom pill did on every click. jsdom does not apply `inert`, so Testing Library still finds the subtree: assert `closest('[inert]')` instead of a missing role.
- The Hive's counts: the Summary counts direct Workers by group, the panel header the whole Hive, and the sidebar glyph direct Workers; "Done" is only a finished Run. The groups and labels themselves were replaced by the next-actor rule (#380, below).
- The Hive panel's default view is a tree (#377): `buildHiveTreeItems` (`features/hive-panel/lib/hive-tree-structure.ts`) flattens the loaded pages into WAI-ARIA `treeitem` rows; the tree query is keyset-paged by `updated_at`, so a parent can arrive on a later page and its child shows at the top level as `Spawned by ⟨parent⟩ (not loaded)` until then. View, Show archived and collapsed rows persist in `useHivePanelViewStore` (`openwaggle:hive-panel:v1`). Every Hive surface names a parent as "Spawned by", never `via`.
- Usage & Cost rows are titled from the Pi file (latest `session_info` name, else the first prompt), because the view runs in the window, whose database is client-isolated: it cannot read OpenWaggle's generated titles without a Host UI channel. The Sessions card counts token use, not spending.
- The Settings statistics switch asks the GUI process for its build or environment opt-out (`app:get-usage-statistics-block`) and shows itself off and disabled with the reason; the saved Setting is untouched.
- In Electron QA, the DevTools MCP writes `take_screenshot` files only inside its workspace roots (the checkout): save under `node_modules/.cache/` and copy to `$OPENWAGGLE_EVIDENCE_DIR`. `scripts/support/session-fixtures.ts` seeds Sessions and Hives into a running QA profile's Host database; reload the page to see them.

### The coordination surface is named `hive` (ADR 0053, October 2026)

The Pi tool, CLI group and MCP tool are `hive`, `openwaggle hive` and `openwaggle_hive` (`@shared/constants/hive`). The former `sessions`, `openwaggle sessions`, `openwaggle delegations`, `openwaggle agents list|search` and `openwaggle_sessions` names are refused, not aliased: the top-level router answers "It is now 'hive'", the Hive CLI and `openwaggle agents` name the replacement subcommand, and an Agent definition listing `sessions` gets `unknown-reference`. A transcript keeps its recorded `sessions` calls; the renderer shows them as an unrecognized (`other`) tool. Each tool action is the `hive` subcommand and MCP operation of the same name with `-` for `_`, so the CLI routes flat names (`delegation-submit`, `queue-withdraw`, `exports-wait`, `authorization-set <id> inherit`), and `mcpSessionCliOptionsV2` passes the MCP operation through as the CLI command unchanged; `hive-action-vocabulary.unit.test.ts` fails if a tool action or MCP operation has no subcommand. Scheduled tasks keep `openwaggle schedules` and have no MCP operation; `openwaggle_hive` also carries `agent-definitions-list|search`, answered in the MCP server process for a project inside the caller's workspace grants (ADR 0053 amendment). Both it and `hive agent-definitions-list|search` apply the project's `agentDefinitionTogglesByProject`, as the tool does, so they never list a definition spawn and launch refuse. `resolveAgentDefinitionProject` (`agent-definition-project.ts`) first maps the caller's directory (CLI `--project`/cwd, MCP grant; as given and its realpath) by the Host's `projectOfWorkspace` rule, shared as `projectOfWorkspacePath` in `adapters/workspace-project-authorization.ts`: an opened project is itself, a ready managed worktree stands for its Session's project, and (discovery only, `insideManagedWorktrees`) so does a directory inside one. A plain subdirectory of a project is its own project, because `create`/`launch` take projectPath as given and an MCP `--workspace /repo/src` grant must not read /repo. Without the worktree mapping a worktree matched no toggle entry. Settings are read first, so a Settings failure lists nothing. The vocabulary test lists these exceptions explicitly. App migration 71 (`hive-tool-name`) rewrites `sessions` to `hive` once in stored `session_execution_profiles.profile_json.tools` and `resolved_agent_snapshot_json.tools` (a fork intersects with the snapshot), because a Session created from a definition listing `sessions` would otherwise silently lose the Hive tool while its identity still lists `sessions:*` capabilities; that is Host-authored data, not an accepted alias. The snapshot's `contentDigest` is a SHA-256 of the parsed definition, so migrated snapshots no longer match it; nothing checks it, and a future integrity check must accept them. Internal module and identifier names (`sessions-tool-*.ts`, `sessions-cli-*.ts`, `SessionsTool*`) were left alone, and so were the `sessions:*` capabilities and the `openwaggle:sessions` export-authorization requester id, because they name the Session concept or persisted keys, not the surface. No contract revision changed: the Host protocol, Session Control/Query v2 and CLI `schemaVersion` 1 payloads are identical; only the front-door names moved.

### Hive tool defaults before 1.0 (issue #352, October 2026)

- A catalog query's `workingPath` (the tool's and CLI's default `current` scope) means the caller's whole project. `resolveCurrentProjectQuery` (`sqlite-session-query-current-project.ts`) rewrites it to `projectPath` in the query repository, after authorization: a query without `projectPath` is unscoped discovery bounded by the caller's scope, so a Worker (reach = its Hive) sees its Hive's Sessions in every worktree of the project. Sending `projectPath` instead would be target-authorized and refused for a Worker (`target_scope_denied`), which is why the tool sends `catalogScope: project` for the caller's own project as `workingPath` too. The mapping matches `projectOfWorkspacePath` with `insideManagedWorktrees`: a ready managed worktree (or a directory inside it) stands for its Session's `project_path`; any other path is itself. The SQL-level `working_path` branches in list/search/delegation queries stay for direct callers but are not reached through the repository.
- The tool and CLI send `archived: false` on `list` by default (the SQL default still lists both, for the renderer). `delegations-list` SQL already listed every state when `states` is empty (`['working']` there only keeps `sql.in` non-empty).
- Hive cleanup kept a Worker the user had visited because `browser-preview-owner-leases.ts` released an owner retained by an active Run only on the next acquire or release, and the Host had no trigger once the Worker was finished. The leases now re-run `releaseIdleOwners` when `activeRunIds` or browser tab groups change, and the background `HiveWorkerCleanup` layer (`hive-worker-cleanup-layer.ts`, `hive-worker-cleanup-retries.ts`) re-checks a Worker kept for desktop state (`open-in-app`, `open-terminal`, `running-service`) on a 15 s to 5 min backoff, bounded to 256 tracked Workers. Every keep reason is logged at info. Evidence: the beta.25 QA Worker's new-worktree child had a visit receipt from during its Run and none after, while the share-parent child was never visited.
- Run settlement no longer turns a Worker's own `needs_attention` into a host-captured submission. "Own" is read durably: `delegation_contracts.attention_cause = 'worker_request'` (since #380) and the latest transition, to `needs_attention`, was written at or after the settling Run's `session_runs.created_at` (a later Run, after the parent answered, settles as usual). A Host-set `needs_attention` still resolves to review when a later Run completes with text, and since #380 it is cleared when the next Run activates anyway.
- Queue outcomes renamed `followUpIds` to `remainingFollowUpIds` and a withdraw adds `withdrawnFollowUpIds` (kept through `deliverAfterQueueDecision`). Stored outcomes with `followUpIds` are upgraded in `decodeStoredSessionControlMutationOutcome`, so replays still decode. Clients decode responses exactly, so this and the other additive fields (`queueState`/`pauseReason` on queued Follow-ups, summary `depth`, spawn `title`, list `parentSessionId`) took Local Session protocol revision 27.
- The tool offers only permitted actions; `prepareArguments` (Pi runs it before schema validation) refuses another action with the capabilities it needs, and an unknown action by name. To return the raw arguments untyped, the tool registers as `registerTool<TSchema, unknown>`, and `assertSessionsToolActionArguments` is an assertion function that narrows them. `sessionsToolPromptGuidelines` composes each line from permitted actions only.
- Pi records the assistant message a Stop or interrupt cut off. One that streamed nothing is projected as a raw `custom` node (role null), keeping the Pi tree linkage, so the transcript and `items` show no empty reply.

### The Hive groups Workers by who acts next (ADR 0059, #380, October 2026)

- One rule, `hiveNextActor` (`src/shared/domain/hive-next-actor.ts`), answers `user | parent | worker | none` plus a reason. The renderer calls it through `hiveMemberNextActor` (`queries/session-hive-next-actor.ts`) for the Hive panel's groups and labels, the tree roll-up, the rail dot, the Queen's sidebar line and the Session Summary; the Host calls it for the query outcomes' `nextActor` (`withHiveNextActor` in `sqlite-session-query-next-actor.ts`, applied only when the dispatcher passes `pendingRequests`) and for the tree page's `needsYouCount` (was `attentionCount`). Do not add another "needs the user" predicate; `hiveStateNeedsAttention` and `hiveDelegationGroup` are gone.
- Facts: `delegation_contracts.attention_cause` (migration 74, `run_failed | worker_request`, read only while the state is `needs_attention`; null on old rows reads as `run_failed`); `parentWillAct` is derived at read time (`parentWillActSql`: the parent has `active_run_id`, or a running queue with a pending Follow-up), so "Stuck" is never stored; parked requests come from the in-memory `agent-loop-interaction-broker` (`hivePendingRequestsBySession`; an authorization confirm is an approval, any other non-notify interaction a question). In the window, the live status store wins over the catalog's `pendingInteractionKind`: `awaiting-input` means a request is parked, any other known status means none.
- Clears: `clearRunFailedAttention` runs in the Run lifecycle's `activate` and `startExternal` transactions (every Run passes one), returns the Delegation to `revision_requested` when the Host's failure transition came from it and to `working` otherwise, and the coordinators publish `session-list-changed` for parent and Worker (`publishDelegationAttentionCleared`). `clearWorkerRequestOnParentDelivery` runs in `executeMutation` (follow-up, message, start) and in the operation journal's `complete` (steer, promote, replace) when the caller is `session-agent:<parent>:…`; that clear rides on the delivery's own `session-state-changed`. A revision request cannot reach a `needs_attention` Delegation (it requires `ready_for_review`). Host failures and clears write `delegation_state_transitions` rows authored `session-host`, so `delegations-read` keeps the history.
- Freshness: the Hive queries are read again on `session-state-changed`/`session-list-changed` and on transport `agent_interaction_request`/`agent_interaction_resolved` (`session-hive-events.ts`); the sidebar catalog re-reads a parent's loaded `needs_attention` Workers whenever the parent's state changes (`withStuckCandidateWorkers`), because their `parentWillAct` was read with them.
- Query outcomes are not decoded exactly (`isSessionQueryResponse` is a shallow guard), so the additive `nextActor` kept Local Session protocol revision 27. Mutation outcomes are stored for replay and do not carry it. OpenWaggle shows no desktop notifications or Dock badge, so there was nothing else to gate on Needs you.
- The panel's grouped view is `by-next-actor` in `useHivePanelViewStore`; version 1 of `openwaggle:hive-panel:v1` maps a stored `by-state` to it.

### The Summary's Hive row is one control (#382, October 2026)

- `SessionSummaryLinkSection` (`SessionSummaryPrimitives.tsx`) is the Summary's section-level row for a surface shown elsewhere: header geometry (`min-h-10 px-3`, `text-sm font-medium` title), one full-row `Button` with hover and `focus-visible` background, a pointer cursor, and a trailing `PanelRight` icon whose `title` names the destination. It must hold no other control; a `target`-less row is plain content with no icon (historical Hives). The row's `aria-label` repeats what it shows ("Open Hive panel, spawned by X, 3 direct Workers, 1 needs you"), because the label replaces the content's text for screen readers.
- `SessionSummaryFacts` lays out short facts so they wrap only between items: each item is `flex min-w-0 max-w-full` with a `truncate` text span, and leads with its own `·` separator; the list is shifted left by one separator width inside an `overflow-hidden` box, so no line starts with a dot. Inline text separators (`{' · '}` between `whitespace-nowrap` spans) let a line end or start with a dangling dot and let an item longer than the row overflow.
- jsdom does not activate a `<button>` on Enter or Space, so `fireEvent.keyDown` proves nothing. Keyboard activation tests use `@testing-library/user-event` (`userEvent.setup()`, `user.tab()`, `user.keyboard('{Enter}')`), a devDependency added for this.
- The Summary is `w-75` (300px) with `max-w-full`, and it is hidden while the Right panel is open, so at the 800px minimum window it is still 300px wide. QA narrower Summaries by emulating the viewport over CDP (`emulate` with `viewport`): with the default 316px sidebar the Summary is the viewport width minus 392px. During the #382 QA, a utility class used for the first time in a live edit (`self-start`) did not apply until `pnpm dev:debug` was restarted; restart before judging a layout that depends on a new class.
- Before #382, every navigational Summary row was already a whole-row button (`SessionSummaryRow`, the Scheduled `TaskRow`, `ChangeRequestLinkRow`, the Environment and Branch triggers); the Hive row was the only one with a small trailing text button.

### Website screenshots are captured, never made by hand (#372, October 2026)

- `scripts/capture-website-screenshots.ts` had stopped running (the header's `Toggle diff panel` button is now `Changes`, Workers left the sidebar for the Hive panel), so later images were made by hand with other fixtures: `hive-sessions.png` showed the retired Hive groups, and `feature-extensible.png` showed the maintainer's real global MCP servers, because `launchOpenWaggleElectron` gave the app a scratch user data directory but the real `HOME`, Pi agent directory and keychain. It now always sets `HOME`, `USERPROFILE` and `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` to scratch (default `<userData>/home`, created before launch; a fixture `environment` cannot override them) and passes `--use-mock-keychain` (plus `--password-store=basic` on Linux), which also covers `packaged-syntax:smoke`. `hero-screenshot.png` and `feature-extensible.png` had been unreferenced since #234 and were removed.
- The script writes exactly `WEBSITE_SCREENSHOTS` (`website-screenshot-manifest.ts`; `--only a,b` captures some) into a staging directory, and `publishScreenshots` copies them over `website/public/screenshots/` only after every selected capture and the renderer-error check succeeded, so a failed run changes no website image. Two tests hold the line: the manifest equals `website/public/screenshots/`, and `website/src/lib/__tests__/screenshot-references.test.ts` requires every shipped image to be referenced with alt text, every reference to exist, and Hive alt text and captions to use the next-actor group names. A screenshot used only in `seo.ts` would fail it; give it a page.
- Fixture data only, in a scratch root made with `mkdtemp` under `/tmp`, not `$TMPDIR`: its paths show in Settings > Worktrees and an agent shell's TMPDIR is a long per-Session path. Real `git worktree add` under `<scratch HOME>/.openwaggle/worktrees/<project>/<id>` gives Settings > Worktrees and the Hive's Workspace column managed worktrees. `seedHive` takes `parentWorkerIndex` (a Worker spawned by an earlier Worker, depth from its parent, a completed parent Run for `parent_run_id`) and `attentionCause`; a `needs_attention` Worker with `run_failed` and an idle parent is Needs you (stuck). Sources & outputs needs no resource rows: transcript parts in `content_json` (`tool-result` for `web_search` and `read`, a user Markdown link, an assistant Markdown image whose file is under the Session's working path) are backfilled by the Host once the app reads the Session's resources. Scheduled tasks go through `window.api.executeScheduledTasks({ operation: 'create' })`, which refuses with an `effect: 'rejected'` outcome rather than throwing. Seeded Hives have no `spawn` operation rows, so Hive cleanup never archives the accepted Worker.
- An assistant's inline Markdown image of a local file renders as `[Image: alt]` text in the transcript, with the captured copy in the message's image group above it. That is the app's current behavior, not a fixture bug.
- The Browser preview guest is a native `WebContentsView` that a hidden window's `page.screenshot()` leaves blank, so the capture composites the guest page's own Playwright screenshot (found through `app.windows()` by URL) at the view's `getBounds()` with `sharp`, after checking the sizes match; the renderer and guest originals and the bounds go to the evidence directory. The site is a loopback `node:http` server the capture starts and stops.
- The GUI's Session Host is detached and survives `app.close()`, so the script ends with `stopElectronQaSessionHost(userData)` before deleting the profile; when that fails it keeps the scratch profile and exits 1 rather than delete files a live Host holds open. A whole run takes about 30 s.

### Scheduled tasks (ADR 0057, ADR 0058, October 2026)

Heartbeats and Launch schedules are Host-only SQLite records (`scheduled_tasks`, `scheduled_task_runs`, migration 70), served by the `scheduled-tasks-v1` Local Session contract (revision 26) and handled in `application/scheduled-task-commands.ts`, never through `authorizeLocalSessionCommand`'s contract path (`classifyLocalSessionPayload` treats it as GUI-only so a probe can never pass). The scheduler (`session-host/scheduled-task-scheduler.ts`, started from `runtime-host-services.ts`) ticks every 30 s, fires due tasks one at a time, and holds a `scheduled-task` liveness owner while any task is enabled; that kind is idle-clock-neutral and is excluded from `hasBlockingDrainOwners`, so a stop or update still drains the Host. A run acts as the task's creator: `resolveScheduledTaskCaller` rebuilds the local user, refreshes a `profile:` caller, or calls `resolveSessionToolAgentCaller` with the stored working directory, and the request goes through `dispatchNonHostUiLocalSessionCommand` (wrapped in `dispatchScheduledTaskCommand` for typing), so authorization, ceilings and reach are the creator's own. Creating a task authorizes by probing that same request (`authorizeScheduledTaskTarget`). Provenance is Host-authored: the run row's `idempotency_key` equals the Session Control request key, and `loadRunExecutionProfile` joins `session_runs.intent_json.idempotencyKey`/`callerId` to it to add `scheduledTask` to the Run identity context; the prompt text is never wrapped. A Heartbeat fires only into an idle Session (no active Run, no Follow-ups, `pi` CLI not last writer); otherwise `markScheduledTaskWaiting` keeps `next_run_at` in the past and the scheduler rechecks every 60 s in memory. Bookkeeping writes use `updated_at` as an optimistic version (`applyWithRetry`); a user reschedule (new `anchor_at`) wins over a concurrent firing's next-time plan. `schedules:read`/`schedules:manage` are in `DEFAULT_SESSION_AGENT_CAPABILITIES` but not in `GRANTABLE_CHILD_CAPABILITIES`, so Workers never get them. Background start writes a LaunchAgent (macOS), XDG autostart entry (Linux, under `app.getPath('appData')`) or login item (Windows) that runs `<app> -- host start`; the OS registration is the setting.

- A Host that dies while a run is `dispatching` must not leave it to re-fire: the next tick would send the same firing again under a new idempotency key and launch a second Session. On start, `settleInterruptedRuns` joins the run's `(caller_id, idempotency_key)` to `session_operations`, which Session Control and Session lifecycle write in the same transaction as the Run or Session they start, so a row there means the request was delivered; the run settles started (or refused) and the task moves past that firing (`scheduled-task-interrupted-runs.ts`). No row means nothing was sent, and the task stays due for an ordinary catch-up.
- The GUI has no Scheduled tasks page. A task opens where the user is (`useShowScheduledTasks`): the Right panel's `scheduled-tasks` workspace index surface when a chat in its project is open, otherwise Settings › Scheduled tasks. Each container keeps its own view (`list | task | edit | new`) in `scheduled-tasks-navigation-store`, and the editor replaces the detail in place. The Session Summary's `scheduled` section lists the Session's Heartbeats or the Launch schedule that started it. The schedule math (`task-schedule`, recurrence rules) lives in `src/shared/domain/scheduled-tasks` so the editor's next-runs preview applies the Host's own rules. Components the shell renders without a QueryClient (the panel title) read the derived `scheduled-task-index-store`, not the query.
- Task times are presented to the second with the UTC offset on every surface (#355): `zonedTimestamp` (`YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS ±HH:MM`, `@shared/utils/zoned-time`) backs the hive tool result (`sessions-tool-schedule-result.ts`) and `cliWallClockTime` (which appends the zone name); the renderer's `formatAbsoluteTime` uses `timeZoneName: 'shortOffset'` even on the machine's own zone. Minutes alone made a jittered 09:58:59 look 2 minutes early, and without the offset the two occurrences of a repeated DST hour read the same. `isTaskScheduleJittered` (task-schedule.ts) decides the jitter note shown wherever `nextRunAt` is (`nextRunJitter` in the tool, `(±5 min)` in `schedules` and `status`, `± up to 5 min` in the app); it anchors at `nextNominalAt ?? createdAt` because `ScheduledTask` does not expose `anchor_at`. The note's text comes from `SCHEDULED_TASK_JITTER_MINUTES`/`SCHEDULED_TASK_JITTER_TEXT`. `ScheduledTaskStatusSummary.jittered` is an additive status field. A `runs-listed` page names no zone, so human `openwaggle schedules runs` reads the task (`read`) for its zone and falls back to the machine's; `--json` sends only the `runs` request. App absolute times use the task's zone when it differs from the machine's (`differingTimeZone`), including the Session Summary tooltip. Every last-run surface names its trigger (`schedule`/`catch-up`/`run-now`), so a manual run never reads as a misfire. Enable switches are named for their checked meaning (`Task enabled`, `<name> enabled`), never an action like "Pause", which a screen reader would read as "Pause, on".
- MCP clients cannot manage Scheduled tasks: `openwaggle mcp serve` authenticates as `transient-mcp:<digest>`, an authority that ends with the process, so no run could revalidate it. The MCP adapter rejects `scheduled-tasks-v1` with an explicit message instead of exposing operations the Host would always refuse.
- `missedOccurrences` counts at most 1000 firings (about 3.5 days of a 5-minute Heartbeat), but must still return the true latest occurrence, which the catch-up run names; it finds it by an expanding look-back window from the end.
- Reading a timestamp CLI usage error by its message words mislabels it (`Missing …` became `not_found`); argument errors throw `CommandCliUsageError`, which `isCommandCliUsageError` recognizes by type.
- Heartbeat efficiency (#388, ADR 0057 amendment, migration 75 `heartbeat-efficiency`, Local Session revision 28): `scheduled_tasks` gained `stop_when`, `indefinite`, `idle_pause_after`, `consecutive_idle_runs`, `wake_check_*` and `auto_pause_*`; `scheduled_task_runs` gained `wake_check`, `outcome*` and `context_json`. Heartbeat-only state is presented as `task.heartbeat` (null for a Launch schedule) and written by `writeHeartbeatColumns` after the main insert/update, in the same transaction. The rule that every Heartbeat has a way to finish is `heartbeatHasStopCondition` (`@shared/domain/scheduled-tasks/heartbeat-rules`), checked by the Host (`stopConditionRefusal`, also on update) and early by the tool, CLI and editor; MCP cannot create tasks at all. The migration marked only Heartbeats with no `ends_at`/`max_runs` as indefinite.
- The Wake check runs inside `fireScheduledTask` after the idle-Session precheck (`scheduled-task-heartbeat-gate.ts`), before any run row, so a Host that dies mid-check just rechecks at the next tick. The runner is a hook (`ScheduledTaskHooks.runWakeCheck`, `adapters/scheduled-tasks/wake-check-process.ts`) installed by the scheduler; it reuses Project actions' `processCommand` (shell choice) and `getInteractiveTerminalEnv`, spawns without a PTY so stdout and stderr stay apart, leads its own process group (POSIX) and kills the group 500 ms after the shell exits and at the 30 s timeout (taskkill `/t` on Windows), then stops waiting 500 ms later even if a descendant holds the pipe. `decideWakeCheck` is pure: idle only for exit 0 and blank stdout; failure for a start error, timeout, signal, or 126/127. Stderr with exit 0 is idle on purpose (tools print progress there). The scheduler loop is serial, so a slow check delays later due tasks by up to 30 s.
- Approval is Host state, not a Pi Authorization request: `wake_check_approved_command` must equal the command; `pending_since` marks a hold. A local-user principal setting a command approves it; others' new commands are pending when `sessionAuthorizationMode(target, creator)` (hook over `resolveEffectiveAuthorizationMode`) is `ask-for-approval`, re-evaluated at each firing (a YOLO Session clears a stale hold). A held Heartbeat waits via `waitForSession(..., 'wake-check-unapproved')`, like a busy Session, so the scheduler rechecks every 60 s with no process spawn. `approve-wake-check` refuses profile callers; a session-agent caller is accepted because only the hive tool's post-`ctx.ui.confirm` path sends it (no tool action exposes it; precedent: export-write authorization is enforced in tool code). The editor sends `heartbeat.wakeCheck` only when the user changed it, so saving another edit never approves a pending command.
- Run outcome: `report-outcome` takes the Session Run id (`durableSessionRunId`), finds its run via `scheduled_task_runs.run_id`, and accepts only that Run's own Session (or the local user). A reported outcome replaces an earlier one; an inferred one only fills a blank. `done` auto-pauses at report time; idle/acted are applied once when the Run ends, through `ScheduledTaskHooks.heartbeatRunEnded`, which `session-control-run-dispatch.ts` calls for Heartbeat Runs with the Run's tool-call parts and the scheduler settles in a forked effect (`settleHeartbeatRunOutcome`). Inference uses `HEARTBEAT_READ_ONLY_TOOLS`/`HEARTBEAT_READ_ONLY_HIVE_ACTIONS`; `bash` and `codemode` always count as acted. A failed or aborted Run with no report changes nothing. `runCount` still counts started runs only, so skipped checks never use up `maxRuns`.
- The Run identity's Heartbeat fields and its per-task model come from the run row's `context_json` (`HeartbeatRunContext`, decoded with an exact schema; an unreadable one is dropped with a warning, the Run still starts), joined in `loadRunExecutionProfile`. The task model overrides `execution.model`; its thinking level reaches Pi as `AgentRunInput.thinkingLevel`, which `runAgentKernel` applies by overlaying `executionThinkingLevel` on the Session detail, so the Session's profile is never written. Renderer strings must not contain `#388`-style issue refs: the design-token lint reads `#388` as a raw hex color.

### What the window reads of a Session is bounded (v1 performance B5/D5/B7, October 2026)

- `sessions:get-tree` returns `SessionTree` with content-free `SessionTreeNode`s (`preview`, `hasMessage`; `contentJson` only for agent-loop audit nodes). The Host and main-process code use `SessionContentTree`/`SessionContentWorkspace` (full `SessionNode`s); the views are built only at the Host UI boundary (`shared/utils/session-data-view.ts`, `host-ui-session-data-operations.ts`). Never read `node.message` from a tree in the renderer; a message's id is its node's id, so use `hasMessage`.
- The workspace path is a window (`transcriptWindow.startIndex > 0` means the Host holds older entries). Anything that needs the whole active path derives it from the tree's parent ids (`buildPiTranscriptPath`), never from `transcriptPath` (`session-tree-panel-rows`, `workspacePathNodeIds`). One-off `api.getSessionWorkspace(id, selection)` calls without a request still get the whole path.
- Reuse depends on `session_control_states.node_mutation_revision`, read before the nodes. Sessions without a control-state row report null and nothing is reused. Appends do not bump it; updates, deletes and inserts into history do (snapshot renumbering of audit nodes does too, so a Run usually resends).
- `api.getSessionDetail` in the renderer is wrapped (`shared/lib/session-detail-reader.ts`): callers always get a complete detail, unchanged messages keep identity. `querySessionControl` joins identical queries made in the same task. Tree/workspace reads coalesce in `session-workspace-reads.ts`: same-task calls join, later calls share one trailing read.
- Project Actions queries are never polled on a timer: runs/preparation poll only while active (or while the scope Session's agent works); everything refetches on focus, and on Host `session-state-changed`/`session-list-changed` only for the changed Sessions' scopes and projects, never discovery (`isProjectActionQueryAffected`). A changed Session the window has not loaded (created in the same batch, a Worker beyond the loaded page) has no known project, so that batch reads every project's non-discovery queries (V1 P3-5). Starting/stopping a run invalidates them.
- The tree filter's Host search (`sessions:search-tree`) returns a cursor (last created order read, node mutation revision); the next search for the same query passes it and, while the revision holds, reads only the nodes appended since (`incremental`). Without it, a query left open read and folded the whole Session's `content_json` on every append of a Run (V2 P3-1).
- A Session open must read one detail and one workspace, nothing else (W10). The detail is bounded to the newest 400 messages (`maxMessages`, `messagesWindow`); never assume `activeSession.messages` is the whole history (count with `messagesWindow.pathLength`, find older messages in the workspace). An unbounded 11 MB detail cost ~500 ms in transport alone: the Host reads and hydrates 3,600 messages in ~20 ms, but frames over 8 MB go out as base64 chunks the main process parses. Duplicate reads came from the read receipt's Host event (now `catalogOnly`), from `refreshSession` refreshing the tree after an open, and from a tree read racing the route's workspace read. Diagnose with an IPC timeline (start/end per invoke on the main clock, aligned to the click) before profiling the renderer.
- Measuring: a large fixture with hundreds of markdown links per message keeps `sessions:resources:backfill` busy for ~600 ms per page on the Host for minutes; use a link-free fixture to measure open latency. Re-opening a large Session is dominated by renderer layout (composer auto-resize `scrollHeight`, transcript `getBoundingClientRect`), not IPC.

### A full outbound budget used to close whichever Host connection sent next (October 2026)

- "Local Session Host connection closed." from a live Host (same PID, no crash or stall logged) was the Host's global outbound byte budget (256 MB, every encode reserving 72 MB) running out while large answers waited unread, typically the desktop main process parsing a 10–20 MB Session detail. The next sender on *any* connection then failed with `LocalSessionOutboundCapacityError` and its socket was destroyed silently: GUI Host UI reads, the desktop poll and the renderer event stream all dropped together, and the GUI's one replay hit the same full budget. Reproduced against a real Host by opening nine connections that request a 13 MB detail and never read; a following `sessions:pins:list` failed in 2 ms.
- The budget now runs encodes in arrival order; one that finds no room waits for a release. After `outboundCapacityWaitMs` (4 s) from its arrival it closes a connection that left a frame unread for 4 s (since its last written slice: frames are written in ≤1 MiB slices so slow but steady readers show progress), preferring one whose bytes alone free enough room, else the one holding most, and waits up to 500 ms for that connection's own bytes before choosing another. It closes none when the stopped connections together could not make room. Otherwise it waits for a release or for a reader to stop, and fails the asker with `LocalSessionOutboundCapacityError` only at `MAX_OUTBOUND_CAPACITY_WAIT_MS` (8 s wall from when the frame was ready), after closing as many stopped readers as it takes in one last 500 ms pass. Do not count that limit from command receipt: Host UI reads (`sessions:get-detail` included), waits and Waggle commands have no client timeout, so a slow command would lose its answer and connection to a full budget at once.
- All of these times are live time (`LocalSessionHostLiveClock`): a 25 ms ticker runs while any frame is held, and time the event loop was blocked (a gap between ticks beyond 50 ms) is not counted, since no socket could be served.
